\nu_{b}
, at which

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}[\eta=1;\,\nu=\nu_{a}]={\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}[\eta=1;\,\nu=\nu_{b}]=1\;.

(123)

One of these special values is
\nu_{a}=0
, as anticipated from
(
115
) and (
504
).
The second special value is always given by
\nu_{b}=2
or
\nu_{b}=-2
.
This is fully consistent with our main claim in (
92
), since the SCI of a 3D
{\cal N}=4
rank-0 SCFT is known to possess precisely two such distinguished limits: the A-twisting and B-twisting points. In these limits, the SCI takes the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{SCI at the A-twisting point :}\;\;{\cal I}^{A}_{\rm sci}(q):=\Tr_{{\cal H}_{\rm rad}(S^{2})}(-1)^{R_{A}}\,q^{\frac{R_{A}}{2}+j_{3}},\\
&\text{SCI at the B-twisting point :}\;\;{\cal I}^{B}_{\rm sci}(q):=\Tr_{{\cal H}_{\rm rad}(S^{2})}(-1)^{R_{B}}\,q^{\frac{R_{B}}{2}+j_{3}},\\
&\text{where}\quad R_{A}=2J_{3}^{H},\qquad R_{B}=2J_{3}^{C}\;.\end{split}

(124)

At these twisting points, contributions to the index arise only from operators on the Coulomb or Higgs branch, along with their descendants.
Because rank-0 SCFTs have trivial Coulomb and Higgs branches by definition, the superconformal index in both limits reduces to
1
.

Comparing (
118
) with (
124
), we obtain the identification

\displaystyle R_{\rm geo}=R_{A}\;,\qquad\frac{\nu_{b}}{2}\times\left(T:=\bm{v}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}\right)=(T_{A}:=J_{3}^{C}-J_{3}^{H})

(125)

When

\left(Q\not\equiv\pm 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,P)\right)\;\land\;\left(P\not\equiv\pm 1\,(\mathrm{mod}\,Q)\right),

we instead find four special points,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})=\bm{\nu}_{aa},\ \bm{\nu}_{ab},\ \bm{\nu}_{ba},\ \bm{\nu}_{bb},\\
&\textrm{satisfying }\bm{\nu}_{ba}-\bm{\nu}_{aa}=\bm{\nu}_{bb}-\bm{\nu}_{ab},\quad\bm{\nu}_{ab}-\bm{\nu}_{aa}=\bm{\nu}_{bb}-\bm{\nu}_{ba}\end{split}

(126)

at which

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}\big[\eta_{1}=\eta_{2}=1;\,(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})\big]\Big|_{(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})=\bm{\nu}_{aa},\,\bm{\nu}_{ab},\,\bm{\nu}_{ba},\,\bm{\nu}_{bb}}=1\;.

(127)

One of these special values is
\bm{\nu}_{aa}=(0,0)
, as anticipated from
(
115
) and (
504
). This is again consistent with our claim in (
92
), which identifies these four points with the

(A,A)
,
(A,B)
,
(B,A)
, and
(B,B)
twisting limits of the product of two rank-0 SCFTs. Comparing (
118
) with (
124
), we obtain the identification

\displaystyle\begin{split}&R_{\rm geo}=R^{(1)}_{A}+R^{(2)}_{A}\;,\;\;\frac{\bm{\nu}_{ba}\cdot(T_{1},T_{2})}{2}=T_{A}^{(1)},\;\;\frac{\bm{\nu}_{ab}\cdot(T_{1},T_{2})}{2}=T_{A}^{(2)}\\
&\textrm{where }T_{I}:=\bm{v}_{I}\cdot\mathbf{T}\;.\end{split}

(128)

Example :
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]
with
(P,Q)=(2,7)

Using the matrices
(K,\mathbf{Q})
in (
2.2
), the index for
T[A,B]
theory is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[A,B]}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4};\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3},\mu_{4}](q)\\
&=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{4}\oint_{|u_{i}|=1}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}u_{1}^{6m_{1}+2m_{3}}u_{2}^{m_{2}-m_{3}+m_{4}}u_{3}^{2m_{1}-m_{2}+2m_{3}-m_{4}}u_{4}^{m_{2}-m_{3}+2m_{4}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left(\prod_{i=1}^{4}(x_{i}(-q^{1/2})^{\mu_{i}})^{m_{i}}\right){\cal I}_{\Delta}(2m_{1},u_{1}^{2}){\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{2},u_{2}){\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{3},u_{3}){\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{4},u_{4})\end{split}

(129)

Then, the index for
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]
is

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}[u,\eta;\mu_{m},\nu]={\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[A,B]}[\bm{x};\bm{\mu}](q)\bigg|_{*}

(130)

where the replacement is (using the NZ matrices in (
29
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\nu\bm{v}_{1},\;x_{i}\rightarrow u^{(\bm{v}_{m})_{i}}\eta^{(\bm{v}_{1})_{i}}\textrm{ with }\\
&\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}=(0,-1,0,0)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}_{m}=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\cdot(1,0,0,0)^{T}=(0,-1,0,-1)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}_{1}=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\cdot(0,1,0,0)^{T}=(-1,0,-1,0)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(131)

Computing the index in
q
-expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}[u,\eta;\mu_{m},\nu]=\bigg(1+(\eta-1)q+\left(\eta+\frac{1}{\eta}-2\right)q^{2}+(\eta-1)q^{3}\\
&\qquad\quad+\left(\eta^{2}-\eta-\frac{1}{\eta}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)q^{4}+\left(4+\eta^{2}-3\eta-\frac{3}{\eta}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)q^{5}+\ldots\bigg)\bigg|_{\eta\rightarrow\eta(-q^{1/2})^{\nu}}\end{split}

(132)

Note that the index is independent on
(u,\mu_{m})
, which is compatible with that the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry decouples in the IR. The index becomes trivial when

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}[u,\eta=1;\mu_{m},\nu=0]={\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}[u,\eta=1;\mu_{m},\nu=2]=1\;.

(133)

Two special points,
\nu=\nu_{a}=0
and
\nu=\nu_{b}=2
, can be regarded as the
A-twisting and B-twisting limits, respectively.
At
\nu=\nu_{\rm con}=1
, the index computes the superconformal index using the
superconformal
R
-charge and coincides with the index of the

\mathcal{T}_{r=2}
theory studied in
Gang:2023rei
.
This theory is a rank-
0
SCFT that supports the rational VOA

\mathcal{M}(2,7)
at the boundary.
At the superconformal point
\nu=1
, the index contains the following term:

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}[\eta,\nu=1]\;\supset\;-(\eta+\eta^{-1})\,q^{3/2}.

(134)

Such a contribution is consistent with the presence of conserved current
multiplets associated with enhanced supersymmetry, although by itself it should
be regarded as suggestive rather than definitive evidence
Cordova:2016emh
;
Evtikhiev:2017heo
.

Example :
T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]
with
(P,Q)=(2,7)

Using the matrices
(K_{\rm r},\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r})
in (
2.3
), the index for
T[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r}]
theory is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r}]}[x_{1},x_{2};\mu_{1},\mu_{2}](q)\\
&=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\oint_{|u_{i}|=1}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}u_{1}^{-m_{1}-2m_{2}}u_{2}^{-2m_{1}-3m_{2}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{2}(x_{i}(-q^{1/2})^{\mu_{i}})^{m_{i}}\right){\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{1},u_{1}){\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{2},u_{2})\;.\end{split}

(135)

Then, the index for
T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]
is

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}[\eta;\nu]={\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r}]}[\bm{x};\bm{\mu}](q)\bigg|_{*}

(136)

where the replacement is (using the NZ matrices in (
88
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}+\nu\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1},\;x_{i}\rightarrow\eta^{(\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1})_{i}}\textrm{ with }\\
&\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}=B^{-1}_{\rm(r)}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x;{\rm r}}=(1,3)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1}=B^{-1}_{\rm r}\cdot(1,0)^{T}=(-1,-2)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(137)

Computing the index in
q
-expansion, we obtain the same index in (
132
), as expected from (
74
) and the index becomes trivial when

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}(u,\eta=1;\mu_{m},\nu=0)={\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]}(u,\eta=1;\mu_{m},\nu=2)=1\;.

(138)

Two two special points,
\nu=\nu_{a}=0
and
\nu=\nu_{b}=2
, can be regarded as A-twisting and B-twisting limits.

Example :
T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]
with
(P,Q)=(5,7)

Using the matrices
(K_{\rm r},\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r})
in (
98
), the index for
T[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r}]
theory is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r}]}[x_{1},x_{2},x_{3};\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3}](q)\\
&=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2},m_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint_{|u_{i}|=1}\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}\right)u_{1}^{2m_{1}}u_{2}^{-3m_{2}-2m_{3}}u_{3}^{-2m_{2}-m_{3}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}(x_{i}(-q^{1/2})^{\mu_{i}})^{m_{i}}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{1},u_{1}){\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{2},u_{2}){\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{3},u_{3})\;.\end{split}

(139)

Then, the index for
T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(5,7)}]
is

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(5,7)}]}[\eta_{1},\eta_{2};\nu_{1},\nu_{2}]={\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r}]}[\bm{x};\bm{\mu}](q)\bigg|_{*}

(140)

where the replacement is (using the reduced NZ matrices in (
97
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}+\nu_{1}\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1}+\nu_{2}\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{2},\;x_{i}\rightarrow\eta_{1}^{(\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1})_{i}}\eta_{2}^{(\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{2})_{i}}\textrm{ with }\\
&\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}=B^{-1}_{\rm(r)}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x;{\rm r}}=(-2,3,1)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1}=B^{-1}_{\rm r}\cdot(1,0,0)^{T}=(1,0,0)^{T},\quad\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{2}=B^{-1}_{\rm r}\cdot(0,1,0)^{T}=(0,-2,-1)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(141)

Computing the index in
q
-expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(5,7)}]}[\eta_{1},\eta_{2};\nu_{1},\nu_{2}](q)=\\
&\bigg(1+\big(\eta_{1}-1\big)q+\big(\eta_{1}+\frac{1}{\eta_{1}}-2\big)q^{2}+\big(\eta_{1}+\frac{1}{\eta_{1}}-2\big)q^{3}+\big(\eta_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta_{1}}-2\big)q^{4}+(\eta_{1}^{2}-\eta_{1})q^{5}+\ldots\bigg)\\
&\times\bigg(1+(\eta_{2}-1)q+\big(\eta_{2}+\frac{1}{\eta_{2}}-2\big)q^{2}+(\eta_{2}-1)q^{3}+\big(\eta_{2}^{2}-\eta_{2}-\frac{1}{\eta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{2}^{2}}\big)q^{4}\\
&\qquad+\big(4+\eta_{2}^{2}-3\eta_{2}-\frac{3}{\eta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta_{2}^{2}}\big)q^{5}+\ldots\bigg)\bigg|_{\eta_{1}\rightarrow\eta_{1}(-q^{1/2})^{\nu_{1}},\;\eta_{2}\rightarrow\eta_{2}(-q^{1/2})^{\nu_{2}}}\end{split}

(142)

The index exhibits a factorization property, as expected from
(
92
). The index becomes trivial (
=1
) when
\bm{\eta}=\bm{1}
and

\bm{\nu}
takes one of the following four values:

\displaystyle\bm{\nu}_{aa}=(0,0),\qquad\bm{\nu}_{ba}=(2,0),\qquad\bm{\nu}_{ab}=(0,2),\qquad\bm{\nu}_{bb}=(2,2)\;.

(143)

3.3
Squashed 3-sphere partition function

The squashed three-sphere partition function
Kapustin:2009kz
;
Hama:2011ea
;
Dimofte:2011ju
;
Gang:2019jut
is an important RG-invariant BPS
partition function of three-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theories. It is, in many respects, a more refined invariant than the superconformal index,
since it is sensitive to decoupled topological sectors that may be invisible in
the index. At
b=1
, the partition function plays a central role in determining the
infrared superconformal
R
-charge via
F
-maximization
Jafferis:2010un
.
Combined with the superconformal index computation, this information is crucial
for analyzing the structure of superconformal multiplets at the IR fixed point. Moreover, the squashed-sphere partition function provides a non-trivial
dictionary of the 3D–3D correspondence, which will be crucial for identifying the correct bottom-up
field-theoretic construction of the three-dimensional theory
T[M]
.

The partition function for
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]
and
T[A,B]
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal Z}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]=\int\frac{d^{r}\mathbf{Z}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{r/2}}{\cal I}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu})\;,\\
&\textrm{ with }{\cal I}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\bm{\zeta},\bm{\mu}):=\exp\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}^{T}\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Z}+2\mathbf{Z}^{T}\mathbf{W}}{2\hbar}\right)\prod_{i}\psi_{\hbar}(\sum_{j}Q_{ij}Z_{j})\bigg|_{\mathbf{W}=\bm{\xi}+(i\pi+\frac{\hbar}{2})\bm{\mu}}\\
&{\cal Z}^{T[A,B]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]=\sqrt{N}{\cal Z}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]\;.\end{split}

(144)

Here we define
\hbar:=2\pi ib^{2}
and
\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{r})^{T}
.
\psi_{\hbar}
denotes the quantum dilogarithm function defined in (
486
). The vector
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{r})^{T}
denotes the (rescaled) FI parameters for the
U(1)^{r}
gauge symmetry, which are equivalent to real masses for the
U(1)^{r}_{\rm top}
symmetry. The partition function
{\cal Z}^{T[A,B]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]
is equivalent to the partition function for
g\cdot(T_{\Delta})^{\otimes r}
in (
5
)
Dimofte:2011ju
;
Dimofte:2012qj
. In the above, we use the fact that
{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b}}[{\cal A}^{N,p}]=1/\sqrt{N}
.

The partition function for
T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}]
(resp.
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
) can be obtained from
{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b}}^{T[A,B]}
(resp.
{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b}}^{{\mathbb{T}}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}
) by specializing
\bm{\xi}\in\mathfrak{F}[\mathcal{T}]
in (
47
) and
\bm{\mu}\in\mathfrak{M}[{\cal T}]
in (
106
). Using the basis in (
49
), the partition functions for
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]
and
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
can be given as:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]:={\cal Z}^{T[A,B]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]\big|_{*}\quad\bigl(\bm{\zeta}:=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{\sharp_{H}})^{T},\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{\sharp_{H}})^{T}\bigr)\;,\\
&{\cal Z}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]:={\cal Z}^{\mathbf{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]\big|_{*}\;,\\
&\textrm{with the replacement }\big|_{*}:\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\nu_{I}\bm{v}_{I},\quad\bm{\xi}\rightarrow\zeta_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\zeta_{I}\bm{v}_{I}\;.\end{split}

(145)

\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}\in\mathfrak{M}[{\cal T}]
is given in (
107
).
(\zeta_{m},\mu_{m})
can be regarded as the (rescaled real mass, R-symmetry mixing parameter) for the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry while the
(\bm{\zeta},\bm{\nu})
are that for other flavor symmetries.
Especially by turning off the all the parameters except for the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry, we have (after rescaling the integral variables
\mathbf{Z}\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{Z}
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}=\mathbf{0}]={\cal Z}^{T[A,B]}_{S^{3}_{b}}\big[\bm{\xi}=\zeta_{m}\bm{v}_{\mathbf{m}},\bm{\mu}=\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{\mathbf{m}}\big]\\
&=\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\det\mathbf{Q}}\int\frac{d^{r}\mathbf{Z}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{r/2}}\exp\bigg(\frac{\mathbf{Z}B^{-1}A\mathbf{Z}+(2\pi i+\hbar)\mathbf{Z}^{T}B^{-1}\bm{\nu}_{x}}{2\hbar}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{2(\zeta_{m}+(i\pi+\frac{\hbar}{2})\mu_{m})\mathbf{Z}^{T}B^{-1}(1,\ldots,0)^{T}}{2\hbar}\bigg)\prod_{i=1}^{r}\psi_{\hbar}(Z_{i})\;.\end{split}

(146)

Comparing with the state-integral expression in (
485
), and using the relation in (
32
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}=\mathbf{0}]=Z^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=1}}_{M=S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}}[X_{m}=\zeta_{m}+(i\pi+\frac{\hbar}{2})\mu_{m}]\;.

(147)

This agrees exactly with the 3D–3D relations in (
9
), (
10
), and (
11
). Such a highly non-trivial consistency check justifies our definition of
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]
in (
30
), (
36
), and (
43
), as well as the identifications in (
51
) and (
110
).

Torus knot case

For the case when
M=S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}
, the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry decouples in the IR and the partition function is expected to be independent on the
(\zeta_{m},\mu_{m})
modulo a phase factor:

\displaystyle{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]\simeq{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu}]\;.

(148)

The partition function for the simplified UV field theory
T^{(\rm r)}_{\rm DGG}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]
in (
73
) is (note that
\sharp_{H}^{(\rm r)}=\sharp_{H}
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal Z}^{T^{(\rm r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu}]:={\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b}}^{T[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r}]}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]\big|_{*}\\
&\textrm{with the replacement }\big|_{*}:\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}+\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\nu_{I}\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I},\quad\bm{\xi}\rightarrow\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\zeta_{I}\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I}\;.\end{split}

(149)

Here
\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I}
and
\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}
are defined in (
77
) and (
120
) respectively. Two theories are IR equivalent up to an decoupled TQFT (
74
), we expect their partition functions should agree up to a contribution from the decoupled TQFT:

\displaystyle{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu}]\simeq{\cal Z}^{T^{(\rm r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu}]\times{\cal Z}_{S^{3}}[\textrm{Abelian TQFT}]\;.

(150)

Using the basis
\{\bm{v}_{I}\}
of
\mathfrak{F}[{\cal T}]
in (
69
), the partition function is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu}]\\
&=\begin{cases}{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}},&|P-Q|=1,\\[3.00003pt]
{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta;\nu],&\left(Q\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ P)\right)\ \oplus\ \left(P\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ Q)\right)\\[3.00003pt]
{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2};\nu_{1},\nu_{2}],&\left(Q\not\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ P)\right)\;\land\;\left(P\not\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ Q)\right).\end{cases}\end{split}

(151)

When
|P-Q|=1
, we found that the partition function is independent of the squashing parameter
b
,
up to an overall phase, and its magnitude is given by

\displaystyle{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}\;\simeq\;S_{00}\big(\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\big)=\sqrt{\frac{8}{PQ}}\,\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi Q}{P}\right)\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi P}{Q}\right).

(152)

This result is compatible with the claim in
(
92
), together with the dictionary of the
bulk–boundary correspondence
zbMATH04092352
. Checking the
b
-independence is highly nontrivial, since performing the full localization integral is generically very difficult.
Instead, one can verify the independence perturbatively by expanding the integral in powers of
(b-1)
using the method in
Gang:2019jut
, and checking the independence order by order.

When

\left(Q\equiv\pm 1\;(\mathrm{mod}\;P)\right)\oplus\left(P\equiv\pm 1\;(\mathrm{mod}\;Q)\right),

we find that there exist two special values of
\nu
, denoted
\nu=\nu_{a}
and

\nu=\nu_{b}
, for which the partition function becomes
b
-independent when
\zeta=0
:

\displaystyle{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b}}^{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}[\zeta=0;\nu]\Big|_{\nu=\nu_{a}\ \mathrm{or}\ \nu_{b}}\quad\text{is $b$-independent modulo a phase.}

(153)

These two points coincide with the special values identified in the
superconformal index analysis of (
123
):
one is always
\nu_{a}=0
, while the other is
\nu_{b}=\pm 2
.
They correspond to the A- and B-twisting choices, respectively.
Accordingly, from the claim in (
92
), we expect

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\textrm{at }\nu=\nu_{a}=0\textrm{ or }\nu=\nu_{b},\\
&{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\zeta=0;\nu]\;\simeq\;S_{00}\big(\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\big)=\sqrt{\frac{8}{PQ}}\,\sin\left(\frac{\pi Q}{P}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\pi P}{Q}\right).\end{split}

(154)

If the point is at
\nu=\nu_{a}
(respectively,
\nu_{b}
), we expect that the

r
-VOA of
{\cal M}(P,Q)
is realized in the topologically A-twisted
(respectively, B-twisted) theory.
We further expect that, at
b=1
, the absolute value of the partition function is
minimized at

\nu=\nu_{\rm con}:=\frac{\nu_{b}}{2}.

This follows from the fact that the squashed
S^{3}_{b}
partition function is computed
using the R-charge

R_{\nu}=R_{A}+\nu T=2J_{3}^{H}+\frac{2\nu}{\nu_{b}}\,(J_{3}^{C}-J_{3}^{H}),

see (
125
), and that

|{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}|
is minimized at the superconformal
R-charge
R_{\rm con}=J_{3}^{H}+J_{3}^{C}
, according to the F-maximization principle

Jafferis:2010un
.

When

\left(Q\not\equiv\pm 1\;(\mathrm{mod}\;P)\right)\land\left(P\not\equiv\pm 1\;(\mathrm{mod}\;Q)\right),

we find that there exist four special values of

\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
, denoted

\bm{\nu}_{aa},\bm{\nu}_{ab},\bm{\nu}_{ba},\bm{\nu}_{bb}
,
for which the partition function becomes
b
-independent when

\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0}
:

\displaystyle{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b}}^{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}[\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0};\,\bm{\nu}]\Big|_{\bm{\nu}=\bm{\nu}_{aa},\ \bm{\nu}_{ba},\ \bm{\nu}_{ab},\ \bm{\nu}_{bb}}\quad\text{is $b$-independent modulo a phase.}

(155)

These four points coincide with the special values obtained from the superconformal
index analysis in (
127
); one of them is always

\bm{\nu}_{aa}=\mathbf{0}
.
They correspond to the
(\mathrm{A},\mathrm{A})
-,
(\mathrm{B},\mathrm{A})
-,

(\mathrm{A},\mathrm{B})
-, and
(\mathrm{B},\mathrm{B})
-twisting choices of the two
rank-0 SCFT factors. From (
92
), we expect that there exists a choice

\bm{\nu}\in\{\bm{\nu}_{aa},\,\bm{\nu}_{ba},\,\bm{\nu}_{ab},\,\bm{\nu}_{bb}\}

for which

\displaystyle{\cal Z}^{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}_{S^{3}_{b}}[\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0};\,\bm{\nu}]\;\simeq\;S_{00}\big(\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\big)=\sqrt{\frac{8}{PQ}}\,\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi Q}{P}\right)\sin\!\left(\frac{\pi P}{Q}\right).

(156)

Furthermore, we expect that at
b=1
the absolute value of the partition function is
minimized at

\bm{\nu}_{\rm con}:=\frac{\bm{\nu}_{ba}+\bm{\nu}_{ab}}{2}.

This follows from the fact that the squashed
S^{3}_{b}
partition function is computed
using the R-charge

\displaystyle R_{\bm{\nu}}=R^{(1)}_{A}+R^{(2)}_{A}+\nu_{1}T_{1}+\nu_{2}T_{2}=2J_{3}^{(1)H}+2J_{3}^{(2)H}+\nu_{1}T_{1}+\nu_{2}T_{2}

(157)

and that the superconformal R-charge

R_{\rm con}=J_{3}^{(1)H}+J_{3}^{(1)C}+J_{3}^{(2)H}+J_{3}^{(2)C}

is equal to
R_{\bm{\nu}=\bm{\nu}_{\rm con}}
,
see (
128
).

Example :
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]
with
(P,Q)=(2,7)

Using the matrices
(K,\mathbf{Q})
in (
2.2
), the integrand for
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
theory is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta_{m},\zeta;\mu_{m},\nu)\\
&=\exp\left(\frac{6Z_{1}^{2}+Z_{2}^{2}+4Z_{1}Z_{3}+2Z_{4}^{2}+2(Z_{2}-Z_{3})(Z_{4}-Z_{3})+2\sum_{i=1}^{4}Z_{i}\big(\xi_{i}+(\pi i+\hbar/2)\mu_{i}\big)}{2\hbar}\right)\\
&\quad\times\psi_{\hbar}(2Z_{1})\psi_{\hbar}(Z_{2})\psi_{\hbar}(Z_{3})\psi_{\hbar}(Z_{4})\bigg|_{*}\;,\end{split}

(158)

where the replacement is (using the NZ matrices in (
29
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\nu\bm{v}_{1},\;\bm{\xi}\rightarrow\zeta_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\zeta\bm{v}_{1}\textrm{ with }\\
&\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}=(0,-1,0,0)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}_{m}=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\cdot(1,0,0,0)^{T}=(0,-1,0,-1)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}_{1}=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\cdot(0,1,0,0)^{T}=(-1,0,-1,0)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(159)

A direct evaluation of the localization integral is difficult.
However, for
b=1
, corresponding to the round three-sphere,
the
S^{3}_{b}
partition function can be computed using an alternative approach,
namely the Bethe-sum formula, which will be discussed in
Section
3.4
.
The resulting partition function is independent of

(\zeta_{m},\mu_{m})
, as expected from the decoupling of
U(1)_{m}
.
The function evaluated at
\zeta=0
is shown in
Figure
3
.

Figure 3
:

Absolute value of the round
S^{3}
partition function for

T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)=(2,7)}]
, computed using the
Bethe-sum formula in (
190
).
The partition function attains its minimum at

\nu=\nu_{\rm con}=1
, where the value is

\frac{2}{\sqrt{7}}\sin\!\big(\frac{6\pi}{7}\big)\approx 0.327985
.
At
\nu=0
, the value

\frac{2}{\sqrt{7}}\sin\!\big(\frac{2\pi}{7}\big)\approx 0.591009

matches
|S_{00}|
of the minimal model
{\cal M}(2,7)
.
At
\nu=2
, the value is

\frac{2}{\sqrt{7}}\sin\!\big(\frac{4\pi}{7}\big)\approx 0.736976
.

Example :
T^{\rm(r)}[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]
with
(P,Q)=(2,7)

Using the matrices
(K_{\rm r},\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r})
in (
2.3
), the integrand for
\mathbb{T}^{\rm(r)}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
theory is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{\mathbb{T}^{\rm(r)}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta;\nu)\\
&=\exp\left(\frac{-Z_{1}^{2}-3Z_{2}^{2}-4Z_{1}Z_{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{2}Z_{i}\big(\xi_{i}+(\pi i+\hbar/2)\mu_{i}\big)}{2\hbar}\right)\psi_{\hbar}(Z_{1})\psi_{\hbar}(Z_{2})\bigg|_{*}\;,\end{split}

(160)

where the replacement is (using the NZ matrices in (
88
))

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}+\nu\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1},\;\bm{\xi}\rightarrow\zeta\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1}\textrm{ with }\\
&\bm{\mu}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}=B^{-1}_{\rm(r)}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x;{\rm r}}=(1,3)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1}=B^{-1}_{\rm r}\cdot(1,0)^{T}=(-1,-2)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(161)

3.4
Twisted Partition functions

Twisted partition functions
Benini:2015noa
;
Benini:2016hjo
;
Closset:2016arn
;
Closset:2017zgf
;
Closset:2018ghr
provide very useful BPS invariants, especially in
situations where the theory flows in the infrared to a unitary TQFT or to a
three-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
rank-
0
superconformal field theory.
They allow one to probe basic data of the TQFTs appearing in the IR, including
both unitary TQFTs and non-unitary TQFTs arising after topological twisting. In particular, twisted partition functions can be used to extract part of the
modular data of the resulting topological field theories.
This information characterizes the modular transformation properties of the
characters of boundary rational vertex operator algebras (r-VOAs).

Here we compute the twisted partition functions for the theory
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
defined in
(
44
).
This theory differs from
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]
only by a decoupled
topological quantum field theory,

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]\simeq T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]\otimes\mathcal{A}^{N,p}\,,

(162)

where
\mathcal{A}^{N,p}
is an Abelian TQFT.
In the special case
N=1
, equivalently

\left|\det\mathbf{Q}/\sqrt{\det B}\right|^{2}=1
,
the decoupled sector is trivial, and the two theories coincide.

A convenient way to compute the twisted partition function is to consider
the asymptotic expansion of the integrand
{\cal I}_{\hbar}
of the squashed
three-sphere partition function in the limit

\hbar:=2\pi ib^{2}\to 0

Gang:2019jut
. The integrand is given by

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}):=\bigl({\cal I}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu})\ \text{in~\eqref{S3-b for tT[A,B;Q]}}\bigr)\Big|_{*},

(163)

where the replacement
\big|_{*}
is defined in
(
145
). Then,
{\cal W}_{0}
and
{\cal W}_{1}
are defined through the asymptotic expansion

\displaystyle\log{\cal I}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu})\xrightarrow{\;\hbar\to 0\;}\frac{{\cal W}_{0}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu})}{\hbar}+{\cal W}_{1}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu})+O(\hbar),

(164)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal W}_{0}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu})&\simeq\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{Z}^{T}\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{Z}^{T}(\bm{\xi}+i\pi\bm{\mu})\Big|_{*}+\sum_{i}{\rm Li}_{2}\!\left(e^{-\sum_{j}Q_{ij}Z_{j}}\right),\\[4.0pt]
{\cal W}_{1}(\mathbf{Z};\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu})&\simeq\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{Z}^{T}\bm{\mu}\Big|_{*}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}\log\!\left(1-e^{-\sum_{j}Q_{ij}Z_{j}}\right).\end{split}

(165)

We use the following asymptotic behavior of the quantum dilogarithm function:

\displaystyle\log\psi_{\hbar}(Z)\simeq\frac{{\rm Li}_{2}(e^{-Z})}{\hbar}-\frac{1}{2}\log\!\left(1-e^{-Z}\right)+O(\hbar),\qquad\text{as }\hbar\to 0.

(166)

Here the equivalence relation
\simeq
is defined as

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{0}

\displaystyle\simeq{\cal W}_{0}+2\pi i\,\mathbf{Z}\cdot\mathbf{n}+4\pi^{2}l,\qquad(\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r},\;l\in\mathbb{Z}),

(167)

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{1}

\displaystyle\simeq{\cal W}_{1}+\pi i\,\mathbb{Z}.

(168)

We then define the Bethe vacua, fibering operator, and handle-gluing operator as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{Bethe vacua :}\quad{\rm BV}&=\Bigl\{\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{r})\;\big|\;\exp\!\left(\partial_{Z_{i}}{\cal W}_{0}\right)\Big|_{\mathbf{Z}\to\log\mathbf{z}}=1,\;i=1,\ldots,r\Bigr\},\\[4.0pt]
\text{Fibering operator :}\quad{\cal F}&=\exp\!\left(-\frac{{\cal W}_{0}-\mathbf{Z}\cdot\partial_{\mathbf{Z}}{\cal W}_{0}-\zeta_{m}\partial_{\zeta_{m}}{\cal W}_{0}-\bm{\zeta}\cdot\partial_{\bm{\zeta}}{\cal W}_{0}}{2\pi i}\right)\Bigg|_{\mathbf{Z}\to\log\mathbf{z}},\\[6.0pt]
\text{Handle-gluing operator :}\quad{\cal H}&=\det_{i,j}\!\left(\partial_{Z_{i}}\partial_{Z_{j}}{\cal W}_{0}\right)\,e^{-2{\cal W}_{1}}\Big|_{\mathbf{Z}\to\log\mathbf{z}}.\end{split}

(169)

More explicitly, the Bethe-vacua equations take the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{BV equations :}\quad\prod_{j}z_{j}^{K_{ij}}\bigl(1-z_{j}^{-Q_{ij}}\bigr)&=\exp\!\left(-\xi_{i}-i\pi\mu_{i}\right)\Big|_{*},\qquad i=1,\ldots,r,\\
\text{with}\;\;K&=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\mathbf{Q}.\end{split}

(170)

Let
\mathbf{HF}
denote the set

\displaystyle\mathbf{HF}(\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}):=\bigcup_{\mathbf{z}\in{\rm BV}}({\cal H},{\cal F}).

(171)

Using this set, the twisted partition function on
{\cal M}_{(g,p)}
—the degree-
p

circle bundle over a genus-
g
Riemann surface
\Sigma_{g}
—is given by

Closset:2017zgf

\displaystyle{\cal Z}_{{\cal M}_{(g,p)}}=\sum_{({\cal H},{\cal F})\in\mathbf{HF}}{\cal H}^{\,g-1}\,{\cal F}^{\,p}.

(172)

In this construction, we turn on suitable magnetic fluxes along

\Sigma_{g}
for the
U(1)_{R}
symmetry. Due to the Dirac quantization
condition, the set
\mathbf{HF}
is well defined only when the

R
-symmetry mixing parameters are integers, namely

\displaystyle\bm{\mu}=\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\nu_{I}\bm{v}_{I}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\;.

(173)

The twisted partition function depends on the choice of spin structure
on
{\cal M}_{(g,p)}
. The formula above corresponds to the spin structure
with anti-periodic boundary conditions along the
S^{1}
fiber.
Such a spin structure exists only when
p
is even, and therefore the
above expression is valid only for even
p
. For this reason, it is more natural to introduce the modified set

\displaystyle\mathbf{HF}^{2}(\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}):=\bigcup_{\mathbf{z}\in{\rm BV}}({\cal H},{\cal F}^{2})\;,

(174)

which can be used to compute
{\cal Z}_{{\cal M}_{(g,p)}}
for all even

p\in 2\mathbb{Z}
. Alternatively, one may consider the set
\mathbf{HF}
associated with
periodic boundary conditions along the
S^{1}
fiber in order to compute
the twisted partition function for odd
p
.
It turns out that the sets
\mathbf{HF}
defined using anti-periodic
and periodic boundary conditions coincide provided that the following
condition is satisfied:

\displaystyle K_{ii}+\bm{\mu}_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z},\qquad\forall\,i=1,\ldots,r\;.

(175)

When this condition holds, the twisted partition function

{\cal Z}_{{\cal M}_{(g,p)}}
can be computed using
\mathbf{HF}
for arbitrary

p\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Otherwise, one must restrict to the set
\mathbf{HF^{2}}
, and the
partition function is well defined only for even values

p\in 2\mathbb{Z}
.

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{HF}\\
\mathbf{HF^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\textrm{ is well-defined when }\begin{pmatrix}K_{ii}+\mu_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\\
\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{pmatrix}\;\;\forall i=1,\ldots,r\;.

(176)

From (
162
), the set
\mathbf{HF}
(or
\mathbf{HF^{2}}
) is expected to be factorized as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\mathbf{HF}\textrm{ of }\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}])=(\mathbf{HF}\textrm{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}])\times(\mathbf{HF}\textrm{ of }\mathcal{A}^{N,p})\;.\end{split}

(177)

For topological field theories or rational conformal field theories,
the sets
\mathbf{HF}
and
\mathbf{HF}^{2}
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{HF}(\text{TQFT or RCFT})&\;\simeq\;\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}}\left(S_{0\alpha}^{-2},\;T_{\alpha\alpha}\right),\\[4.0pt]
\mathbf{HF}^{2}(\text{TQFT or RCFT})&\;\simeq\;\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}}\left(S_{0\alpha}^{-2},\;T_{\alpha\alpha}^{2}\right).\end{split}

(178)

Here
\mathcal{I}
labels the set of simple objects in the TQFT,
or equivalently the set of primary fields in the corresponding RCFT.
The matrices
(S,T)
denote the modular
S
- and
T
-matrices,
and the label
\alpha=0
corresponds to the identity operator.
The symbol
\simeq
denotes equality up to an overall phase
in the identification of the
\mathcal{F}
s.
More precisely,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigl(\mathbf{HF}=\{({\cal H}_{\alpha},{\cal F}_{\alpha})\}\bigr)\;\simeq\;\bigl(\widetilde{\mathbf{HF}}=\{(\tilde{{\cal H}}_{\alpha},\tilde{{\cal F}}_{\alpha})\}\bigr)\end{split}

(179)

if and only if

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal H}_{\alpha}=\tilde{{\cal H}}_{\alpha},\qquad{\cal F}_{\alpha}=e^{i\delta}\,\tilde{{\cal F}}_{\alpha},\qquad\forall\,\alpha\in\mathcal{I},\end{split}

(180)

for some real phase
\delta
independent of
\alpha
.

Torus knot case

The set
\mathbf{HF}
is independent of
(\zeta_{m},\mu_{m})
, as expected, because the

U(1)_{m}
symmetry of
T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]
always
decouples in the IR, as stated in (
67
):

\displaystyle\mathbf{HF}(\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu})\simeq\mathbf{HF}(\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu})\;.

(181)

For the simplified UV gauge theory

T^{(\rm r)}[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]
, the set

\mathbf{HF}
can be computed in the same way using the integrand of the

S^{3}_{b}
partition function in (
149
).
Since the the two theories are expected to be IR equivalent up to an decoupled TQFT (
74
), we expect that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bigl(\mathbf{HF}(\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu})\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\\
&\simeq\ \bigl(\mathbf{HF}(\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu})\text{ of }T^{(\rm r)}[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\times(\mathbf{HF}\textrm{ of deocupled Abelian TQFT})\;.\end{split}

(182)

Using the basis
\{\bm{v}_{I}\}
of
\mathfrak{F}[\mathcal{T}]
in
(
69
), the set
\mathbf{HF}
takes the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bigl(\mathbf{HF}(\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu})\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\\
&=\begin{cases}\mathbf{HF},&|P-Q|=1,\\[3.00003pt]
\mathbf{HF}(\zeta;\nu),&\left(Q\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ P)\right)\ \oplus\ \left(P\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ Q)\right),\\[3.00003pt]
\mathbf{HF}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2};\nu_{1},\nu_{2}),&\left(Q\not\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ P)\right)\ \land\ \left(P\not\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ Q)\right).\end{cases}\end{split}

(183)

When
|P-Q|=1
, using (
92
) and
(
176
), together with the bulk–boundary dictionaries

Gang:2021hrd
;
Gang:2023rei
, we expect

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bigl(\mathbf{HF}\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathbf{HF}\text{ or }\overline{\mathbf{HF}}\text{ of }\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\bigr),&\text{if }K_{ii}+(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geom})_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\quad\forall\,1\leq i\leq r,\\[6.0pt]
\bigl(\mathbf{HF}^{2}\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathbf{HF}^{2}\text{ or }\overline{\mathbf{HF}^{2}}\text{ of }\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\bigr),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(184)

Here we define

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbf{HF}}:=\left(\text{the complex conjugate of }\mathbf{HF}\right).

(185)

This computes the
\mathbf{HF}
associated with the parity-conjugate theory.
In the construction of
T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}]
, we are not careful about the
choice of orientation of the knot complement.
As a result,
\mathbf{HF}
may be identified with its complex conjugate

\overline{\mathbf{HF}}
, which corresponds to reversing the orientation of the
internal three-manifold.

When

\bigl(Q\equiv\pm 1\!\!\!\pmod{P}\bigr)\ \oplus\ \bigl(P\equiv\pm 1\!\!\!\pmod{Q}\bigr),

we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{HF}_{\rm a}&:=\mathbf{HF}(\zeta;\nu)\Big|_{\zeta=0,\ \nu=\nu_{a}},\\
\mathbf{HF}_{\rm b}&:=\mathbf{HF}(\zeta;\nu)\Big|_{\zeta=0,\ \nu=\nu_{b}},\end{split}

(186)

where
\nu_{a}=0
and
\nu_{b}=\pm 2
, as determined by the criterion
in (
123
).
We then expect

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{if }K_{ii}+(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geom})_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\qquad\forall\,1\leq i\leq r,\\[4.0pt]
&\bigl(\mathbf{HF}_{\rm a}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}_{\rm b}\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathbf{HF}\text{ or }\overline{\mathbf{HF}}\text{ of }\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\bigr),\\[6.0pt]
&\text{otherwise},\\[4.0pt]
&\bigl(\mathbf{HF}^{2}_{\rm a}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}^{2}_{\rm b}\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathbf{HF}^{2}\text{ or }\overline{\mathbf{HF}^{2}}\text{ of }\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\bigr).\end{split}

(187)

When

\bigl(Q\not\equiv\pm 1\!\!\!\pmod{P}\bigr)\ \land\ \bigl(P\not\equiv\pm 1\!\!\!\pmod{Q}\bigr),

we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{HF}_{\rm aa/ba/ab/bb}:=\mathbf{HF}(\bm{\zeta};\bm{\nu})\Big|_{\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0},\ \bm{\nu}=\bm{\nu}_{\rm aa}/\bm{\nu}_{\rm ba}/\bm{\nu}_{\rm ab}/\bm{\nu}_{\rm bb}}\;.\end{split}

(188)

Here
\bm{\nu}_{\rm aa}=\mathbf{0}
, and the remaining

\bm{\nu}_{*}
are defined in (
127
).
We then expect

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{if }K_{ii}+(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geom})_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\qquad\forall\,1\leq i\leq r,\\[4.0pt]
&\bigl(\mathbf{HF}_{\rm aa}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}_{\rm ba}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}_{\rm ab}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}_{\rm bb}\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathbf{HF}\text{ or }\overline{\mathbf{HF}}\text{ of }\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\bigr),\\[6.0pt]
&\text{otherwise},\\[4.0pt]
&\bigl(\mathbf{HF}^{2}_{\rm aa}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}^{2}_{\rm ba}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}^{2}_{\rm ab}\text{ or }\mathbf{HF}^{2}_{\rm bb}\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathbf{HF}^{2}\text{ or }\overline{\mathbf{HF}^{2}}\text{ of }\mathcal{M}(P,Q)\bigr).\end{split}

(189)

Computation of the round three-sphere partition function from the Bethe sum

If

K_{ii}+(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geom})_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}

for all
i=1,\ldots,r
, the round three-sphere partition function

{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b}}
at
b=1
can be computed using the set
\mathbf{HF}
as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m}=0,\bm{\nu}=\mathbf{0}]&\simeq{\cal Z}_{{\cal M}_{(g=0,p=1)}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m}=0,\bm{\nu}=\mathbf{0}]\\[3.00003pt]
&\simeq\sum_{({\cal H},{\cal F})\,\in\,\mathbf{HF}(\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m}=0,\bm{\nu}=0)}{\cal H}^{-1}\,{\cal F},\\[3.99994pt]
\text{and}\;\;{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}[\zeta_{m},\bm{\zeta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]&={\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}\bigl[\zeta_{m}+i\pi\mu_{m},\;\bm{\zeta}+i\pi\bm{\nu};\;\mu_{m}=0,\;\bm{\nu}=\mathbf{0}\bigr].\end{split}

(190)

If instead

K_{ii}+(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo})_{i}\notin 2\mathbb{Z}

for some
1\leq i\leq r
, the round three-sphere partition function can
still be computed by choosing a reference vector

\bm{\mu}_{0}
satisfying

K_{ii}+(\bm{\mu}_{0})_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}

for all
i=1,\ldots,r
. One then proceeds as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}[\zeta_{m},\zeta;\mu_{m},\nu]={\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]\Big|_{*}\;\text{with the replacement specified in
\eqref{S3b ptn for T_{DGG}}}\;,\\[3.99994pt]
&{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}]={\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}\big[\bm{\xi}+i\pi(\bm{\mu}-\bm{\mu}_{0}),\bm{\mu}_{0}\big]\;,\\[6.00006pt]
&{\cal Z}_{S^{3}_{b=1}}[\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}_{0}]\simeq\sum_{({\cal H},{\cal F})\,\in\,\mathbf{HF}(\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}_{0})}{\cal H}^{-1}\,{\cal F}\;.\end{split}

(191)

Here
\mathbf{HF}(\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu}_{0})
denotes the set of
pairs
({\cal H},{\cal F})
computed from the asymptotic expansion coefficients

{\cal W}_{0}
and
{\cal W}_{1}
of the function

{\cal I}_{\hbar}(\mathbf{Z};\bm{\xi},\bm{\mu})

appearing in (
144
), following the prescription
outlined in (
169
).

Example:
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}]
with
(P,Q)=(2,7)

Using the integrand of
\mathbb{T}^{[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}
in (
158
)
together with the general formula (
169
), one can compute the set

\mathbf{HF}(\zeta_{m},\zeta;\mu_{m},\nu)
.
From the explicit computation, we find:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\rm i)}\quad\mathbf{HF}\text{ is independent of }(\zeta_{m},\mu_{m})\;,\\[3.99994pt]
&{\rm ii)}\quad\mathbf{HF}_{a}:=\mathbf{HF}(\zeta_{m},\zeta=0;\mu_{m}=0,\nu=0)\\[1.99997pt]
&\qquad\simeq\bigg\{\Big(\frac{7}{4\sin^{2}(\tfrac{2\pi}{7})},\;\mathbf{e}(0)\Big),\;\Big(\frac{7}{4\sin^{2}(\tfrac{4\pi}{7})},\;\mathbf{e}(\tfrac{2}{7})\Big),\;\Big(\frac{7}{4\sin^{2}(\tfrac{6\pi}{7})},\;\mathbf{e}(\tfrac{3}{7})\Big)\bigg\}\\
&\qquad\times\bigg\{(2,\mathbf{e}(0)),\;\big(2,\mathbf{e}(\tfrac{3}{4})\big)\bigg\}\;,\\[6.00006pt]
&{\rm iii)}\quad\mathbf{HF}_{b}:=\mathbf{HF}(\zeta_{m},\zeta=0;\mu_{m}=0,\nu=2)\\[1.99997pt]
&\qquad\simeq\bigg\{\Big(\frac{7}{4\sin^{2}(\tfrac{4\pi}{7})},\;\mathbf{e}(0)\Big),\;\Big(\frac{7}{4\sin^{2}(\tfrac{6\pi}{7})},\;\mathbf{e}(\tfrac{4}{7})\Big),\;\Big(\frac{7}{4\sin^{2}(\tfrac{2\pi}{7})},\;\mathbf{e}(\tfrac{6}{7})\Big)\bigg\}\\
&\qquad\times\bigg\{(2,\mathbf{e}(0)),\;\big(2,\mathbf{e}(\tfrac{3}{4})\big)\bigg\}\;.\end{split}

(192)

We define

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}(h):=e^{2\pi ih}\;.

(193)

The property in (i) is consistent with the fact that the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry decouples in
the infrared.
In both
\mathbf{HF}_{a}
and
\mathbf{HF}_{b}
, the first factor corresponds to the

\mathbf{HF}
set of
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]
, while the second factor corresponds
to the decoupled Abelian TQFT
{\cal A}^{N=2,p}
.
Therefore, we confirm that

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{HF}_{a}\text{ of }T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,7)}]\big)\;\simeq\;\big(\overline{\mathbf{HF}}\text{ of }{\cal M}(2,7)\big)\;.

(194)

Using the Bethe-sum formula in (
190
), one can compute the
round three-sphere partition function.
Its absolute value, as a function of
\nu
, is shown in
Figure
3
.

4
Rational VOA characters from Half-indices

Now we compute the half-index
Gadde:2013wq
;
Yoshida:2014ssa
;
Dimofte:2017tpi
of
T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]
, or more precisely, of
\mathbb{T}^{[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}
, which is identical to
T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]
up to a decoupled TQFT
\mathcal{A}^{N,p}
. This will make the direct connection with the boundary rational VOAs more transparent.

Unlike the case of bulk BPS partition functions, the computation of the half-index is more subtle, since one must choose appropriate boundary conditions in order to realize a rational VOA at the boundary. Moreover, a single bulk theory may admit several distinct rational VOAs, depending on the choice of boundary condition. From the UV gauge-theory perspective, it is generally unclear which UV boundary condition flows to a given desired IR boundary condition.

Furthermore, even when the bulk theory factorizes in the infrared as a product,
the half-index does not necessarily exhibit a corresponding factorization.
This is because the choice of boundary conditions can explicitly break the
factorization between the two sectors.
Since such factorization is not manifest in the UV description—and, in
particular, because our choice of Dirichlet boundary conditions on the UV fields
obscures the factorized structure of the IR theory—the decoupling of the Abelian
TQFT
\mathcal{A}^{N,p}
in (
162
) is not automatic at the
level of the half-index computation. For this reason, we focus on the theory

\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
rather than directly on

T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}]
.
This perspective naturally explains how the bulk theory associated with a
minimal model can be realized as a product of rank-0 SCFTs, as proposed in
(
92
).

A naive approach to identifying appropriate boundary conditions is to systematically scan the supersymmetric boundary conditions available in a given UV description, compute the corresponding half-index for each choice, and then identify those boundary conditions for which the resulting half-index reproduces the characters of rational VOAs. In this section, however, we adopt a different strategy. We fix a simple boundary condition—essentially Dirichlet boundary conditions for both vector and chiral multiplets—in the UV gauge theory, and instead vary the duality frame to determine which frame yields half-indices that match rational VOA characters.
In the construction of
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]
, different choices of polarization correspond to different duality frames, related by the basic mirror dualities (
7
). Distinct polarizations lead to different NZ matrices
(A,B)
. The Dirichlet half-index is well defined only when the matrix
B^{-1}A
is positive definite.
We therefore scan over the possible choices of polarization and identify those for which

B^{-1}A
becomes positive definite.
We find that, in any duality frame for which
B^{-1}A
is positive
definite, the Dirichlet half-index consistently yields sensible rational
VOA characters (or trivial results): some reproduce the expected
Virasoro minimal-model characters, while others correspond to different
rational VOAs. A similar strategy was employed in
Gang:2023ggt
.
We now present the main claim of this paper:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\textbf{Conjecture:}\quad&\text{The half-indices in }\eqref{half-indices when |P-Q|=1-2},\;\eqref{half-indices at A/B-twistings-2},\;\eqref{half-indices at AA/AB/BA/BB-twistings-2}\text{ are either }0,\;1,\;\\
&\text{or a character of an RCFT up to an overall factor of }\pm q^{\mathbb{Q}}\;.\end{split}

(195)

We assume that the ideal triangulation of the torus-knot complement
satisfies the conditions stated in (
63
).
The half-indices compute the Dirichlet half-index of

\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
or

\mathbb{T}^{\rm(r)}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r},\{E^{\rm(r)}_{I}\}]

evaluated at the topological twisting points.
The half-index can be
0
when the Dirichlet boundary conditions break supersymmetry,
and it can be
1
when the UV boundary condition becomes trivial topological boundary condition in the infrared.
We claim that, except for these exceptional cases, the theory supports a rational VOA
at the boundary, and the half-index computes its character.
We will explicitly check this claim through concrete examples in Appendix
A
.

Half-index of
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]

For theory
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]
in (
30
), the half-index in
(\mathcal{D},D_{c})
boundary condition
9
9

9

In terms of a 2D
\mathcal{N}=(0,2)
subalgebra of the 3D
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal algebra,
a 3D
\mathcal{N}=2
chiral multiplet
\Phi
decomposes into a 2D
(0,2)
chiral multiplet

\Phi_{\rm 2d}
and a 2D
(0,2)
Fermi multiplet
\Psi_{\rm 2d}
.
The deformed Dirichlet boundary condition
D_{c}
for
\Phi
corresponds to imposing

\Phi_{\rm 2d}=c
at the boundary, with a nonzero constant
c
. Similarly, a 3D
\mathcal{N}=2
vector multiplet decomposes into a 2D
(0,2)
vector multiplet
and a 2D
(0,2)
chiral multiplet under the same subalgebra.
The Dirichlet boundary condition
\mathcal{D}
for the 3D vector multiplet is defined by setting
the 2D
(0,2)
vector multiplet to zero at the boundary.

is given as
Dimofte:2017tpi
;
Jockers:2021omw
;
Gang:2023rei

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm half}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\\
\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K\,\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}\cdot\bm{\mu}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}x_{i}^{-m_{i}}}{\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{r}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\;.

(196)

Here
(q)_{n}
denotes the
q
-Pochhammer symbol,

\displaystyle(q)_{n}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1-q^{i})\;.

(197)

Here
K=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\mathbf{Q}
, and the index is well-defined
when
B^{-1}A>0
.
The half-index for the
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
theory is obtained from that of
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]

by restricting the parameters
\{\bm{x},\bm{\mu}\}
to satisfy conditions in (
104
). Using the basis in (
49
), we consider the following half index

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}_{\rm half}[u,\bm{\eta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]:={\cal I}_{\rm half}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]\big|_{*}\;,\end{split}

(198)

with the replacement
|_{*}
given in (
114
),

\displaystyle\big|_{*}:\bm{\mu}\rightarrow\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\nu_{I}\bm{v}_{I},\quad x_{i}\rightarrow u^{(\bm{v}_{m})_{i}}\prod_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\eta_{I}^{(\bm{v}_{I})_{i}}\;.

Half-index of
\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}

Let
\overline{T}
denote the theory obtained by applying parity conjugation to
T
. In 3D-3D correspondence,
\overline{T[M]}=T[\overline{M}]
, where
\overline{M}
denotes the orientation reversal of
M
. The half-index of
\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}
is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm half}^{\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\\
\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot\overline{K}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}\cdot\overline{\bm{\mu}}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}x_{i}^{m_{i}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\;,\\
&\textrm{with }\overline{K}:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}\mathbf{Q}-K=\mathbf{Q}^{T}(\mathbb{I}-B^{-1}A)\mathbf{Q},\;\overline{\bm{\mu}}:=-\mathbf{Q}^{T}\cdot(1,\ldots,1)^{T}-\bm{\mu}\;.\end{split}

(199)

Under the parity transformation, the effective Chern–Simons data flips sign:

\displaystyle\overline{K}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=-K_{\rm eff}\,,\quad\textrm{where}\;\;\overline{K}_{\rm eff}=\overline{K}-\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{Q}^{T}\mathbf{Q}\;\textrm{ and }K_{\rm eff}=K-\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{Q}^{T}\mathbf{Q}\,.

(200)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm eff}=-\bm{\mu}_{\rm eff}\,,\;\;\textrm{where}\;\;\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm eff}=\overline{\bm{\mu}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{Q}^{T}\cdot(1,\ldots,1)^{T}\textrm{ and }\bm{\mu}_{\rm eff}=\bm{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{Q}^{T}\cdot(1,\ldots,1)^{T}\,.

(201)

See (
3.2
) for the background mixed Chern–Simons levels, which account for the offset between
K_{\rm eff}
and
K
.
Note that
\bm{\mu}
can be regarded as the mixed Chern–Simons coupling
between the
U(1)_{R}
symmetry and the dynamical
U(1)^{r}
gauge symmetry.

The half-index is well-defined when
(\mathbb{I}-B^{-1}A)>0
. Then, the half-index for
\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}
is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}}_{\rm half}[u,\bm{\eta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]:={\cal I}_{\rm half}^{\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]}}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]\big|_{*}\;,\end{split}

(202)

with the replacement
|_{*}
given in (
114
).

Torus knot case

Using the basis

\{\bm{v}_{m},\bm{v}_{I}\}
of
\mathfrak{F}[\mathcal{T}]

in (
65
), the half-indices can be given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}_{\rm half}[\bm{\eta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]={\cal I}^{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}_{\rm half}[u=1,\bm{\eta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]\;,\\
&{\cal I}^{\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}}_{\rm half}[\bm{\eta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]={\cal I}^{\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}}_{\rm half}[u=1,\bm{\eta};\mu_{m},\bm{\nu}]\;.\end{split}

(203)

The
U(1)_{m}
symmetry decouples in the infrared, and we turn off the corresponding fugacity,
u=1
. We nevertheless keep the parameter
\mu_{m}
, which parametrizes the mixing
between the
U(1)_{R}
symmetry and the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry.
All bulk partition functions are independent of
\mu_{m}

(up to an overall phase factor), and one may therefore set
\mu_{m}=0

at the level of bulk observables.
However, the half-index computation is more subtle.
Some imprint of the UV
U(1)_{m}
symmetry can survive in the infrared,
especially when the IR theory contains a decoupled TQFT sector,
on which a discrete subgroup of
U(1)_{m}
may act nontrivially. The parameter
\mu_{m}
can be regarded as specifying a choice of the geometric

R
-symmetry mixed with the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry,

\displaystyle R_{\rm geo}\;\longrightarrow\;R_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\,T_{m}\;,

(204)

where
T_{m}
is the generator of
U(1)_{m}
.
The parameter
\mu_{m}
cannot take arbitrary real values,
since
R_{\rm geo}
must be properly quantized,
as discussed in (
3
). The generator
T_{m}
is embedded into the
SU(2)_{m}
algebra as
J_{3}
,
T_{m}=J_{3}
,
with the normalization
J_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}/2

Gang:2018wek
.
Consequently, the admissible values of
\mu_{m}
are restricted to

\displaystyle\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\;.

(205)

In the following, we will compute the half-index with
\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
turned on.

For the reduced UV gauge theory

\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r},\{E_{I}^{(\rm r)}\}]
in (
73
), the half-index is given by
(note that
\sharp_{H}^{(\rm r)}=\sharp_{H}
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal I}^{\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r},\{E_{I}^{(\rm r)}\}]}_{\rm half}[\bm{\eta};\bm{\nu}]&={\cal I}^{\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}]}_{\rm half}[\bm{x};\bm{\mu}]\Big|_{*},\\
\text{with the replacement }\Big|_{*}:\qquad\bm{\mu}&\;\longrightarrow\;\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\nu_{I}\,\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I},\qquad x_{i}\;\longrightarrow\;\prod_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\eta_{I}^{(\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I})_{i}}\;.\end{split}

(206)

Here
\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I}
is defined in (
77
).
Similarly, for its parity conjugation

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal I}^{\overline{\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r},\{E_{I}^{(\rm r)}\}]}}_{\rm half}[\bm{\eta};\bm{\nu}]&={\cal I}^{\overline{\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}]}}_{\rm half}[\bm{x};\bm{\mu}]\Big|_{*},\\
\text{with the replacement }\Big|_{*}:\qquad\bm{\mu}&\;\longrightarrow\;\bm{\mu}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}+\sum_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\nu_{I}\,\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I},\qquad x_{i}\;\longrightarrow\;\prod_{I=1}^{\sharp_{H}}\eta_{I}^{(\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{I})_{i}}\;.\end{split}

(207)

The case when
|P-Q|=1

We introduce a shorthand notation for the four half-indices:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}]&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\\
\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m})}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;K:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\,\mathbf{Q}\;,\;\;\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\!\left(\bm{\nu}_{x}+(\mu_{m},\mathbf{0}_{r-1})^{T}\right)\;,\\[3.99994pt]
\overline{{\cal I}_{\rm half}}[\mu_{m}]&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\\
\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K}\,\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}(\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}-\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m})}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;\overline{K}:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}\mathbf{Q}-K\;,\;\;\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}:=-\mathbf{Q}^{T}(1,\ldots,1)^{T}-\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}\;,\\[6.00006pt]
{\cal I}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm half}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r-2}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r-2}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K_{\rm r}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\bm{\mu}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r-2}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;K_{\rm r}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}B_{\rm r}^{-1}A_{\rm r}\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\;,\;\;\bm{\mu}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}B_{\rm r}^{-1}\bm{\nu}_{x,{\rm r}}\;,\\[3.99994pt]
\overline{{\cal I}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm half}}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r-2}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r-2}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K}_{\rm r}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{\bm{\mu}}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r-2}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;\overline{K}_{\rm r}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}-K_{\rm r}\;,\;\;\overline{\bm{\mu}}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}:=-\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}(1,\ldots,1)^{T}-\bm{\mu}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}\;.\end{split}

(208)

These are the half-indices for

\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
,

\overline{\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]}
,

\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r},\{E^{(\rm r)}_{I}\}]
, and

\overline{\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r},\{E^{(\rm r)}_{I}\}]}
,
respectively.

We expect that the following half-indices, up to an overall prefactor of the form

\pm q^{\mathbb{Q}}
, provide candidates for characters of rational VOAs:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm half}\,[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]\quad\text{when }B^{-1}A>0\;,\qquad\overline{{\cal I}_{\rm half}}\,[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]\quad\text{when }(\mathbb{I}-B^{-1}A)>0\;,\\
&{\cal I}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm half}\quad\text{when }B_{\rm r}^{-1}A_{\rm r}>0\;,\qquad\overline{{\cal I}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm half}}\quad\text{when }(\mathbb{I}-B_{\rm r}^{-1}A_{\rm r})>0\;.\end{split}

(209)

For a fixed ideal triangulation
\mathcal{T}
of a torus-knot complement,
varying the choice of polarization (
15
) for each tetrahedron
may lead to Neumann–Zagier matrices
(A,B)
that satisfy one of the above positivity
conditions. In the examples we have examined, there are typically several admissible
choices. Although
\mu_{m}
can take arbitrary values in
2\mathbb{Z}
, the resulting
inequivalent half-indices form only a finite set.

The case when

\big(Q\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ P)\big)\oplus\big(P\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ Q)\big)

We define

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal I}^{a/b}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}]&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})^{T}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\\
\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\!\left(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\nu\bm{v}_{1}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\Bigg|_{\nu=\nu_{a/b}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;K:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\,\mathbf{Q}\;,\;\;\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\nu\bm{v}_{1}:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\!\left(\bm{\nu}_{x}+(\mu_{m},\nu,\mathbf{0}_{r-2})^{T}\right)\;,\\[3.99994pt]
\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b}_{\rm half}}[\mu_{m}]&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\\
\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K}\,\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\!\left(\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}-\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}-\nu\bm{v}_{1}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\Bigg|_{\nu=\nu_{a/b}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;\overline{K}:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}\mathbf{Q}-K\;,\;\;\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}:=-\mathbf{Q}^{T}(1,\ldots,1)^{T}-\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}\;,\\[6.00006pt]
{\cal I}^{a/b;(\rm r)}_{\rm half}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r-2}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r-2}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K_{\rm r}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\!\left(\bm{\mu}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}+\nu\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{1}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r-2}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\Bigg|_{\nu=\nu_{a/b}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;K_{\rm r}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}B_{\rm r}^{-1}A_{\rm r}\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\;,\;\;\bm{\mu}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}B_{\rm r}^{-1}\bm{\nu}_{x,{\rm r}}\;,\qquad\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{1}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}B_{\rm r}^{-1}(1,\mathbf{0}_{r-3})^{T}\;,\\[3.99994pt]
\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b;(\rm r)}_{\rm half}}&:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r-2}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r-2}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K}_{\rm r}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\!\left(\overline{\bm{\mu}}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}-\nu\bm{v}^{(\rm r)}_{1}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r-2}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\Bigg|_{\nu=\nu_{a/b}}\;,\\
&\hskip 15.00002pt\text{with }\;\overline{K}_{\rm r}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}-K_{\rm r}\;,\;\;\overline{\bm{\mu}}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}:=-\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}(1,\ldots,1)^{T}-\bm{\mu}^{(\rm r)}_{\rm geo}\;.\end{split}

(210)

Here
\nu_{a}=0
and
\nu_{b}=\pm 2
are the special values defined in (
123
).
These are the half-indices for the four theories evaluated at the
A
- and
B
-twisting limits.
We expect that the following half-indices, up to an overall prefactor of the form
\pm q^{\mathbb{Q}}
,
are candidates for characters of rational VOAs:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{a/b}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]\textrm{ when }B^{-1}A>0\;,\;\;\;\;\;\;\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b}_{\rm half}}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]\textrm{ when }(\mathbb{I}-B^{-1}A)>0\;,\\
&{\cal I}^{a/b;{(\rm r)}}_{\rm half}\textrm{ with }B_{\rm r}^{-1}A_{\rm r}>0\;,\;\;\;\;\;\;\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b;{(\rm r)}}_{\rm half}}\textrm{ with }(\mathbb{I}-B_{\rm r}^{-1}A_{\rm r})>0\;.\end{split}

(211)

Example :
(P,Q)=(2,7)

We use the reduced gluing equations in (
83
) with
\mathbf{X}_{\rm r}=(Z_{1},Z_{3})
and
\mathbf{P}_{\rm r}=(Z_{1}^{\prime\prime},Z_{3}^{\prime\prime})
. Then,
(B^{-1}A)_{\rm r}
is not positive definite but
(\mathbb{I}-B^{-1}A)_{\rm r}
is positive definite. With
\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}=\mathbb{I}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\overline{K}_{\rm r}=\mathbb{I}-(B^{-1}A)_{\rm r}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&2\\
2&4\\
\end{array}\right)\;,\\
&\overline{\bm{\mu}}^{\rm(r)}_{\rm geo}=(-1,-1)^{T}-(B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x})_{\rm r}=(-2,-4)^{T}\;,\\
&\bm{v}_{1}^{(\rm r)}=B_{\rm r}^{-1}\cdot(1,0)^{T}=(-1,-2)^{T}\;,\;\nu_{b}=2\;,\end{split}

(212)

Thus,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\overline{{\cal I}^{\rm a;{(\rm r)}}_{\rm half}}=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\geq 0}\frac{q^{m_{1}^{2}+2m_{1}m_{2}+2m_{2}^{2}+m_{1}+2m_{2}}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}}=q^{-17/42}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,7)}_{(1,1)}\;,\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{\rm b;{(\rm r)}}_{\rm half}}=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\geq 0}\frac{q^{m_{1}^{2}+2m_{1}m_{2}+2m_{2}^{2}}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}}=q^{1/42}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,7)}_{(1,3)}\;.\end{split}

(213)

The case

\bigl(Q\not\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ P)\bigr)\land\bigl(P\not\equiv\pm 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ Q)\bigr)
.

In this case, we work in a duality frame (i.e. a choice of polarization) in which
the factorization property is manifest.

For the theory
\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q},\{E_{I}\}]
, we choose a polarization such that
there exists a charge matrix of the factorized form

\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\oplus\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}
,
with
\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}
of sizes

r_{1}\times r_{1}
and
r_{2}\times r_{2}
, respectively, and

r_{1}+r_{2}=r
,
obeying the relation in (
32
), for which the resulting data

(K,\bm{v}_{m},\bm{v}_{1},\bm{v}_{2})

exhibit the following factorization property
10
10

10

One may relax the condition in the second line and consider the most general case,
including the possibility that one of
r_{1}
or
r_{2}
vanishes.
Even in this situation, we expect that the half-indices evaluated at the four twisting points
serve as candidates for r-VOA characters.
However, since we do not yet have a sufficient number of case studies in the case where the IR theory is given by a product of two rank-
0
SCFTs,
we do not include this possibility among the assumptions of the conjecture
in (
195
).
Our conjecture is primarily motivated by the case studies presented in this paper
and in
Gang:2023ggt
;
Gang:2024loa
, and may therefore require further
refinements or modifications in future work.

:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K=K^{(1)}\oplus K^{(2)},\;\;\bm{v}_{m}=\mathbf{0}_{r_{1}}\oplus\bm{v}^{(2)}_{m}\ \ \text{or}\ \ \bm{v}^{(1)}_{m}\oplus\mathbf{0}_{r_{2}},\\[2.0pt]
&\text{and }\exists\;\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{Q}\text{ such that }\bigl(\bm{v}_{1}+\alpha\,\bm{v}_{m},\ \bm{v}_{2}+\beta\,\bm{v}_{m}\bigr)=\bigl(\bm{v}^{(1)}\oplus\mathbf{0}_{r_{2}},\ \mathbf{0}_{r_{1}}\oplus\bm{v}^{(2)}\bigr),\\
&\text{with }\bm{v}^{(1)}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{1}},\;\;\bm{v}^{(2)}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{2}}.\end{split}

(214)

Here

K:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\,\mathbf{Q}
,
and the vectors

are defined

) by

\mu_{m}\bm{v}_{m}+\nu_{1}\bm{v}_{1}+\nu_{2}\bm{v}_{2}:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}(\mu_{m},\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\mathbf{0}_{r-3})^{T}.

Then, in the case

we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{a/b,(1)}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}]:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{1}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K^{(1)}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(1)}+\mu_{m}\bm{v}^{(1)}_{m}+\nu\bm{v}^{(1)}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{1}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\nu=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ba}\cdot(1,0)^{T}}\;,\\
&{\cal I}^{a/b,(2)}_{\rm half}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{2}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K^{(2)}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(2)}+\nu\bm{v}^{(2)}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{2}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\nu}=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ab}\cdot(0,1)^{T}}\;,\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b,(1)}_{\rm half}}[\mu_{m}]:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{1}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K^{(1)}}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}^{(1)}-\mu_{m}\bm{v}^{(1)}_{m}-\nu\bm{v}^{(1)}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{1}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\nu=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ba}\cdot(1,0)^{T}}\;,\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b,(2)}_{\rm half}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{2}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K^{(2)}}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}^{(2)}-\nu\bm{v}^{(2)}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{2}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\bm{\nu}=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ab}\cdot(0,1)^{T}}\;,\\
&\textrm{with }\overline{K^{(i)}}:=(\mathbf{Q}^{(i)})^{T}\mathbf{Q}^{(i)}-K^{(i)},\quad\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}^{(i)}:=-\mathbf{Q}^{(i)}\cdot(1,\ldots,1)^{T}-\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(i)}\;.\end{split}

(215)

Here

\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}:=\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}\bm{\nu}_{x}=\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(1)}\oplus\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(2)}.

Here
\bm{\nu}_{aa}=\mathbf{0}
, while the remaining

are defined as in (
