).
Similarly, for the case

\bm{v}_{m}=\mathbf{0}_{r_{1}}\oplus\bm{v}^{(2)}_{m}
,
one can define

{\cal I}^{a/b,(1)}_{\rm half}
,

{\cal I}^{a/b,(2)}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}]
,

\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b,(1)}_{\rm half}}
,
and

\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b,(2)}_{\rm half}}[\mu_{m}]
.

For the reduced theory

\mathbb{T}[A_{\rm r},B_{\rm r};\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r},\{E_{I}^{\rm(r)}\}]
,
we choose a polarization such that there exists a factorized charge matrix

\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}=\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}_{\rm r}\oplus\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}_{\rm r}

satisfying the relation in (
32
), for which the resulting data

(K_{\rm r},\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1},\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{2})

obey the following factorization properties (
r-2=r_{1}+r_{2}
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K_{\rm r}=K^{(1)}_{\rm r}\oplus K^{(2)}_{\rm r}\textrm{ and }\;\;\\
&(\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1},\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{2})=(\bm{v}^{(1);({\rm r})}\oplus\mathbf{0}_{r_{2}},\mathbf{0}_{r_{1}}\oplus\bm{v}^{(2);({\rm r})})\textrm{ with }\bm{v}^{(1);({\rm r})}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{1}},\;\bm{v}^{(2);({\rm r})}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{2}},\\[2.0pt]
&\text{where}\;\;K_{\rm r}:=(\mathbf{Q}^{T}B^{-1}A\mathbf{Q})_{\rm r},\;\;\nu_{1}\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{1}+\nu_{2}\bm{v}^{\rm(r)}_{2}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\rm r}^{T}B^{-1}_{\rm r}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\mathbf{0}_{r-4})^{T}.\end{split}

(216)

Then, we define

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{a/b,(1);({\rm r})}_{\rm half}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{1}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K^{(1)}_{\rm r}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(1),({\rm r})}+\nu\bm{v}^{(1);({\rm r})}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{1}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\nu=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ba}\cdot(1,0)^{T}}\;,\\
&{\cal I}^{a/b,(2);({\rm r})}_{\rm half}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{2}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}K^{(1)}_{\rm r}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(2),({\rm r})}+\nu\bm{v}^{(2);({\rm r})}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{1}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\nu=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ab}\cdot(0,1)^{T}}\;,\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b,(1);({\rm r})}_{\rm half}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{1}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{1}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K^{(1)}_{\rm r}}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}^{(1),({\rm r})}-\nu\bm{v}^{(1);({\rm r})}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{1}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\nu=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ba}\cdot(1,0)^{T}}\;,\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{a/b,(2);({\rm r})}_{\rm half}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{2}}\\
\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}_{\rm r}\cdot\mathbf{m}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{r_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{m}^{T}\overline{K^{(1)}_{\rm r}}\mathbf{m}}\,(-q^{1/2})^{-\mathbf{m}^{T}\left(\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}^{(2),({\rm r})}-\nu\bm{v}^{(2);({\rm r})}\right)}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r_{1}}(q)_{[\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}\cdot\mathbf{m}]_{i}}}\bigg|_{\nu=\bm{\nu}_{aa/ab}\cdot(0,1)^{T}}\;,\\
&\textrm{with }\overline{K^{(i)}_{\rm r}}:=(\mathbf{Q}^{(i)}_{\rm r})^{T}\mathbf{Q}^{(i)}_{\rm r}-K^{(i)}_{\rm r},\quad\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\rm geo}^{(i);({\rm r})}:=-\mathbf{Q}^{(i)}\cdot(1,\ldots,1)^{T}-\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}^{(i);({\rm r})}\;.\end{split}

(217)

We expect the following indices to become characters of rational VOAs up to an overall factor
\pm q^{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{\rm a/b,(i)}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]\textrm{ or }{\cal I}^{\rm a/b,(i)}_{\rm half}\textrm{ when }K^{(i)}>0\;,\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{\rm a/b,(i)}_{\rm half}}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]\textrm{ or }\overline{{\cal I}^{\rm a/b,(i)}_{\rm half}}\textrm{ when }\overline{K^{(i)}}>0\;,\\
&{\cal I}^{\rm a/b,(i);({\rm r})}_{\rm half}\textrm{ when }K^{(i)}_{\rm r}>0\;,\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{\rm a/b,(i);({\rm r})}_{\rm half}}\textrm{ when }\overline{K^{(i)}_{\rm r}}>0\;,\end{split}

(218)

Example :
(P,Q)=(5,7)

Using the (
97
) and (
98
), we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbf{Q}^{(1)}_{\rm r}=(1),\quad\mathbf{Q}^{(2)}_{\rm r}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad K^{(1)}_{\rm r}=(2),\quad K^{(2)}_{\rm r}=\begin{pmatrix}-3&-2\\
-2&-1\end{pmatrix},\\
&\bm{\mu}^{(1);{\rm(r)}}_{\rm geo}=(-2),\quad\bm{\mu}^{(2);{\rm(r)}}_{\rm geo}=(3,1)^{T},\quad\bm{v}^{(1);{\rm(r)}}=(1)\quad\bm{v}^{(2);{\rm(r)}}=(-2,-1)^{T}\;,\\
&\overline{K^{(1)}_{\rm r}}=(-1),\quad\overline{K^{(2)}_{\rm r}}=\begin{pmatrix}4&2\\
2&2\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{\bm{\mu}}^{(1);{\rm(r)}}_{\rm geo}=(1),\quad\overline{\bm{\mu}}^{(2);{\rm(r)}}_{\rm geo}=(-4,-2)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(219)

Note that
K^{(1)}_{\rm r}>0
and
\overline{K^{(2)}_{\rm r}}>0
and we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{a,(1);(\rm r)}_{\rm half}=\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{q^{m^{2}+m}}{(q)_{m}}=q^{-11/60}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,5)}_{(1,1)},\quad{\cal I}^{b,(1);(\rm r)}_{\rm half}=\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{q^{m^{2}}}{(q)_{m}}=q^{1/60}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,5)}_{(1,2)}\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{a,(2);(\rm r)}_{\rm half}}=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\geq 0}\frac{q^{2m_{1}^{2}+2m_{1}m_{2}+m_{2}^{2}+2m_{1}+m_{2}}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}}=q^{-17/42}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,7)}_{(1,1)},\\
&\overline{{\cal I}^{b,(2);(\rm r)}_{\rm half}}=\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\geq 0}\frac{q^{2m_{1}^{2}+2m_{1}m_{2}+m_{2}^{2}}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}}=q^{1/42}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,7)}_{(1,3)}\;.\end{split}

(220)

Further examples are provided in Appendix
A
.

5
Discussions and Future Directions

Choices of
\mathbf{Q}

There may exist multiple admissible choices of
\mathbf{Q}
satisfying the relation (
32
).
If two such choices, say
\mathbf{Q}_{1}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{2}
, have the same image in
\mathbb{Z}^{r}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{r}=\mathbf{Q}_{2}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{r}\,,

(221)

then they yield identical half-indices and therefore the same characters.
For a fixed pair
(A,B)
, there can be several inequivalent choices of
\mathbf{Q}
with distinct image sets,
potentially leading to different half-indices.
It would be interesting to classify all admissible
\mathbf{Q}
that produce genuinely different half-indices.

Relation to the work by Gang-Kang-Kim
Gang:2024tlp

In the paper, they argue that

\displaystyle(\textrm{Bulk field theory for $\mathcal{M}(P,Q)$})=T[S^{2}((P,P-R),(Q,S),(3,1))]\;.

(222)

Here
S^{2}\big((p_{1},q_{1}),(p_{2},q_{2}),(p_{3},q_{3})\big)
denotes the Seifert-fibered space over
S^{2}
with three singular fibers.
Two integers
R
and
S
are determined by the relation
PS-QR=1
. We will claim that

\displaystyle T[S^{2}((P,P-R),(Q,S),(3,1))]\simeq T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{P,Q}],

(223)

where
\simeq
denotes the IR equivalence. In general, the theory
T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}]
theory has
SU(2)_{m}
flavor symmetry associated with the knot
{\cal K}
. For torus knots
{\cal K}={\cal K}_{(P,Q)}
, the
SU(2)
flavor symmetry decouples in the IR and it only appears as the background Chern-Simons terms with CS level
-PQ
, which can be seen from the 3D-index of the torus knot compelements in (
504
). Thus, using the Dehn filling operation in 3D-3D correspondence
Gang:2018wek
;
Choi:2022dju
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T[(S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{T}_{(P,Q)})_{k}]\\
&=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}{SU(2)_{k}}\right)/\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}=\frac{\left(T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\otimes SU(2)_{k-PQ}\right)}{\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}},\quad k\in 2\mathbb{Z}\\
\left(\frac{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}{SU(2)_{k}}\otimes U(1)_{\pm 2}\right)/\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}=\frac{\left(T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\otimes SU(2)_{k-PQ}\otimes U(1)_{\pm 2}\right)}{\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}},\quad k\in 2\mathbb{Z}+1\end{cases}\end{split}

(224)

Here
(S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K})_{k}
denotes the closed three-manifold obtained from the knot complement
S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}
by performing Dehn filling with integer slope
k
. The tensoring with
U(1)_{\pm 2}
and the gauging of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
one-form symmetry are required in order to match the squashed three-sphere partition function with the
SL(2,\mathbb{C})
Chern–Simons partition function on the resulting three-manifold, in accordance with the 3D–3D correspondence.

When
k=PQ+3
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T[(S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)})_{k=PQ+3}]\\
&=\begin{cases}\frac{\left(T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\otimes U(1)_{2}\right)}{\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}},\quad PQ\in 2\mathbb{Z}+1\\
\left(\frac{T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]}{SU(2)_{k}}\otimes U(1)_{\pm 2}\right)/\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}=\frac{\left(T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(P,Q)}]\otimes U(1)_{2}\otimes U(1)_{\pm 2}\right)}{\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}},\quad PQ\in 2\mathbb{Z}\end{cases}\end{split}

(225)

Here we use the fact that (pure
\mathcal{N}=2\;SU(2)_{3}
)
\simeq SU(2)_{1}\simeq U(1)_{2}
. Topologically, on the other hand, it is known that

\displaystyle(S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)})_{k=PQ+3}=S^{2}((P,-R),(Q,S),(3,1))\;.

(226)

Thus, to confirm the equivalence in (
223
), we need to check that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T[S^{3}((P,-R),(Q,S),(3,1))]\\
&=\begin{cases}\frac{\left(T[S^{3}((P,P-R),(Q,S),(3,1))]\otimes U(1)_{2}\right)}{\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}},\quad PQ\in 2\mathbb{Z}+1\\
\frac{\left(T[S^{3}((P,P-R),(Q,S),(3,1))]\otimes U(1)_{2}\otimes U(1)_{\pm 2}\right)}{\mathbb{Z}^{[1]}_{2}},\quad PQ\in 2\mathbb{Z}\end{cases}\end{split}

(227)

One can verify the above relation case by case at the level of twisted partition functions, using the 3D–3D relation for twisted partition functions in
Cho:2020ljj
;
Gang:2024tlp
.

Other non-hyperbolic knots and links and higher
N
(number of M5-branes)

We expect that our analysis of torus knots can be extended to a broader class of
non-hyperbolic knots and links.
It would be interesting to determine which rational vertex operator algebras
arise from these more general examples.
Another natural direction is to consider three-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=2

gauge theories obtained from the compactification of the six-dimensional

A_{N-1}

(2,0)
theory, with
N\geq 3
, on torus-knot complements.
A concrete field-theoretic construction of such theories is available via the
algorithm developed in
Dimofte:2013iv
.
Motivated by the results of
Kanade:2022wtm
;
Baek:2025uev
, we expect that these
three-dimensional theories admit boundary rational VOAs described by

W_{N}
minimal models, and that their characters can be constructed directly
from the combinatorial data encoded in the
PGL(N,\mathbb{C})
gluing data
Garoufalidis:2015

of the torus-knot complement.

Haagerup-like non-unitary VOAs from once-punctured torus bundles

Another class of three-manifolds that give rise to rank-
0
SCFTs via the
3D–3D correspondence is provided by once-punctured torus bundles
Terashima:2011qi
;
Gang:2013sqa
, which are
hyperbolic.
Unlike the non-hyperbolic three-manifolds studied in this work, for these
examples the geometric R-symmetry
R_{\mathrm{geo}}
in (
2
) coincides with the
superconformal R-charge, rather than with R-symmetry at A-twisting or B-twisting point. In this case, the theory possesses a
U(1)
symmetry associated with the
puncture on the fiber, which does not decouple in the infrared and instead
corresponds to the
U(1)_{A}
symmetry of the rank-
0
SCFT.
For certain classes of once-punctured torus bundles whose monodromy matrix is
of the form
ST^{k}
, the corresponding rational VOA characters have been
studied in
Gang:2022kpe
;
Gang:2023ggt
.
An intriguing feature of these examples is that the modular data of the
associated rational VOAs provide a non-unitary generalization of the modular
data of the Haagerup TQFT
Evans:2010yr
. It would be interesting to extend this analysis to more general classes of
once-punctured torus bundles and to identify new families of
Haagerup-like non-unitary rational VOAs.

Cobordisms between torus knots and domain walls in 3D TQFTs/2D RCFTs

An interesting direction is to interpret cobordisms between torus-knot
complements as domain walls in the associated three-dimensional TQFTs.
Via the 3D–3D correspondence, such cobordisms define interfaces between the
corresponding three-dimensional theories and descend, in the presence of
boundaries, to conformal defects between the associated two-dimensional RCFTs.
From this perspective, mathematical results on cobordisms
feller2015optimal
;
feller2021genus
;
baader2024minimal
may be leveraged to
understand domain walls in RCFTs, including their action on rational VOA
modules, fusion rules, and modular data.

Refined 3D-index

As seen in the case of torus knots, symmetries that are not manifest from the
six-dimensional viewpoint can emerge in the infrared effective theory.
Exploiting such additional symmetries, one can introduce new refinements of the
superconformal index of
T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}]
, beyond the parameters
associated with the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry.
While the 3D index itself has been extensively studied from various perspectives

Dimofte:2011py
;
Garoufalidis:2016ckn
;
Gang:2018gyt
;
Garoufalidis:2020xec
;
Celoria:2025lqm
;
Agarwal:2022cdm
;
Duan:2022ijp
;
Garoufalidis:2024hoe
,
the refined extensions provide a natural starting point for
further exploration
Refined3DIndex:ToAppear
.

Relation to the “3d modularity”

Our work is motivated by ideas similar in spirit to those of

Cheng:2018vpl
;
Cheng:2024vou
, in that both aim to relate vertex operator algebras
associated with three-manifolds via the 3D–3D correspondence and the
half-index.
However, the precise relationship between the two approaches is not yet clear. In the works, they do not rely on an explicit
three-dimensional field-theoretic analysis.
Instead, they directly relate quantum invariants of three-manifolds, known as
the
\widehat{Z}
-invariants
Gukov:2016gkn
;
Gukov:2017kmk
;
Gukov:2019mnk
, to half-indices and study their nontrivial modular
properties.
The VOAs appearing in their construction are typically non-rational, even for
non-hyperbolic three-manifolds.
This is in contrast to our DGG-based construction, where non-hyperbolic
three-manifolds typically give rise to rational VOAs.
Accordingly, the connection between these VOAs and the rational VOAs obtained
in our framework remains unclear.

Appendix A
Examples

In this section, we compute the half-indices appearing in
(
209
),
(
211
), and
(
218
),
and use them to test the conjecture in (
195
).
In the comparison of
q
-series throughout this section, we adopt the following conventions:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&``="\quad\text{equality verified up to }O(q^{100}),\\
&``\simeq"\quad\text{equality up to an overall factor }q^{\mathbb{Q}},\ \text{verified up to }O(q^{100}).\end{split}

(228)

A.1
The case
(P,Q)=(2,2r-1)
with
r\geq 2

A.1.1
(P,Q)=(2,3)

The torus knot complement can be ideally triangulated with
r=2
tetrahedra. Using SnapPy, the gluing data can be obtained by the following input:

\displaystyle\mathtt{Manifold(^{\prime}3\_1^{\prime}).gluing\_equations\_pgl()}\;,

and the gluing equations are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{1}=Z_{1}+Z_{2}^{\prime},\quad C_{2}=Z_{1}+2Z_{1}^{\prime}+2Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{2}+Z_{2}^{\prime}+2Z_{2}^{\prime\prime},\\
&M=-Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{2}\;.\end{split}

(229)

In the ideal triangulation, there is an edge internal edge
\{E_{I}\}=\{C_{1}\}
.

Among the
3^{2}
possible polarization choices, there are four choices satisfying
the positivity condition

B^{-1}A>0\,,\textrm{ or }(\mathbb{I}-B^{-1}A)>0\;,

while all other choices violate this condition.

i)

The first admissible polarization choice is

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z_{1}^{\prime\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime\prime})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z_{1}^{\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime})^{T}\;.

(230)

The NZ matrices in (
25
) are

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}-1&-1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix},\quad B=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{\nu}_{x}=(1,-1)^{T}\;.

(231)

In the case,
B^{-1}A\in M_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
and
|\det B|=1
, thus we can choose
\mathbf{Q}={\mathbb{I}}
and
N=\left|\frac{(\det\mathbf{Q})^{2}}{\det B}\right|=1
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,3)}]={\mathbb{T}}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]\\
&=U(1)_{K-{\mathbb{I}}/2}^{2}\textrm{ coupled to }2\;\Phi\textrm{s of charge }\mathbf{Q}={\mathbb{I}}\textrm{ with }{\cal W}={\cal O}_{E_{1}}\\
&\textrm{with }K=B^{-1}A=\begin{pmatrix}2&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}\textrm{ and }{\cal O}_{E_{1}}=V_{(1,-1)}\;.\end{split}

(232)

The affine spaces
{\mathfrak{M}}[{\cal T}]
in (
106
) and vector space
{\mathfrak{F}}[{\cal T}]
in (
47
) are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathfrak{M}}[{\cal T}]=\{\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})^{T}:\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}=1\}\;,\\
&{\mathfrak{F}}[{\cal T}]=\{\bm{v}=(v_{1},v_{2})^{T}:v_{1}-v_{2}=0\}\;.\end{split}

(233)

As expected in (
107
) and (
49
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\mu}_{\mathrm{geo}}=B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}=(0,-1)^{T}\in{\mathfrak{M}}[{\cal T}]\;,\\
&{\mathfrak{F}}[{\cal T}]=\mathrm{Span}\{\bm{v}_{m}:=B^{-1}\cdot(1,0)^{T}=(-1,-1)^{T}\}\;.\end{split}

(234)

The superconformal index (
114
) for the theory is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,3)}]}[u;\mu_{m}]=\left.{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{T[A,B]}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]\right|_{*}\textrm{ where}\\
&{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{T[A,B]}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\left({\prod_{i=1}^{2}\oint\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}}\right)u_{1}^{2m_{1}+m_{2}}u_{2}^{m_{1}+m_{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\prod_{i=1}^{2}(x_{i}(-q^{1/2})^{\mu_{i}})^{m_{i}}{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{i},u_{i})\;,\\
&\big|_{*}\;:\;(x_{1},x_{2})\rightarrow\left(\frac{1}{u},\frac{1}{u}\right),\;\;(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\rightarrow(-\mu_{m},-1-\mu_{m})\;.\end{split}

(235)

Using the superconformal index, one can explicitly verify that the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry decouples in the IR — the dependence on
(u,\mu_{m})
identically drops out and the theory flows to a unitary TQFT:

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,3)}]}[u;\mu_{m}]=1\;.

(236)

The half-index in (
208
) is

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]=\sum_{\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},m_{2})^{T}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{2}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{m_{2}+\mu_{m}(m1+m2)}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}}=1\;.

(237)

ii)

The second admissible polarization choice is

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z_{1}^{\prime},Z_{2})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z_{1},Z_{2}^{\prime\prime})^{T}\;.

(238)

The NZ matrices are

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},\quad B=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&-1\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{\nu}_{x}=(-1,-1)^{T}\;.

(239)

In the case, we can choose
\mathbf{Q}={\mathbb{I}}
and
N=1
.
K
,
\bm{\mu}_{\mathrm{geo}}
and
\bm{v}_{m}
are

\displaystyle K=B^{-1}A=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&2\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{\mu}_{\mathrm{geo}}=(-1,0)^{T},\quad\bm{v}_{m}=(1,1)^{T}\;,

(240)

so the present choice of polarization gives the same result with the previous case, up to permutation.

iii)

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z_{1},Z_{2}^{\prime\prime})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z_{1}^{\prime\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime})^{T}\;,

(241)

the NZ matrices are

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},\quad B=\begin{pmatrix}-1&-1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{\nu}_{x}=(1,-2)^{T}\;.

(242)

In the case,
|\det B|=1
, and
B^{-1}A
is integer valued, but not positive definite. Instead,
{\mathbb{I}}-B^{-1}A
is positive definite. Therefore, we can choose
\mathbf{Q}={\mathbb{I}}
,
N=1
and

\begin{gathered}\overline{K}={\mathbb{I}}-B^{-1}A=\begin{pmatrix}2&-1\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad{\cal O}_{E_{1}}=\phi_{1}V_{(0,1)}\,,\\
\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\mathrm{geo}}=-(1,1)^{T}-B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}=(-2,1)^{T}\,,\quad\bm{v}_{m}=B^{-1}\cdot(1,0)^{T}=(-1,0)^{T}\;.\end{gathered}

(243)

The superconformal index (
114
) for the theory is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{\overline{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,3)}]}}[u;\mu_{m}]=\left.{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{\overline{T[A,B]}}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]\right|_{*}\textrm{ where}\\
&{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{\overline{T[A,B]}}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\left({\prod_{i=1}^{2}\oint\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}}\right)u_{1}^{2m_{1}-m_{2}}u_{2}^{-m_{1}+m_{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\prod_{i=1}^{2}(x_{i}(-q^{1/2})^{\mu_{i}})^{m_{i}}{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{i},u_{i})\;,\\
&\left.\right|_{*}:(x_{1},x_{2})\rightarrow\left({u,1}\right),\quad(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\rightarrow(-2+\mu_{m},1)\;.\end{split}

(244)

The dependence on
(u,\mu_{m})
identically drops out, and the theory flows to a unitary TQFT in the IR:

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\mathrm{sci}}^{\overline{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,3)}]}}[u;\mu_{m}]=1\;.

(245)

The half-index in (
208
) is

\displaystyle\overline{{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{2}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot\overline{K}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}-m_{2}-\mu_{m}m_{1}}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}}=0\;.

(246)

iv)

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z_{1}^{\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z_{1},Z_{2})^{T}\;,

(247)

the NZ matrices are

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad B=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{\nu}_{x}=(-1,-2)^{T}\;.

(248)

In the case, we can choose
\mathbf{Q}={\mathbb{I}}
,
N=1
and
{\mathbb{I}}-B^{-1}A
is positive definite.
\overline{K}
,
\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\mathrm{geo}}
and
\bm{v}_{m}
are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\overline{K}={\mathbb{I}}-B^{-1}A=\begin{pmatrix}1&-1\\
-1&2\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{\bm{\mu}}_{\mathrm{geo}}=-(1,1)^{T}-B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}=(1,-2)^{T},\\
&\bm{v}_{m}=B^{-1}\cdot(1,0)^{T}=(0,1)^{T}\;,\end{split}

(249)

so the present choice of polarization gives the same result with the previous case, up to permutation. We will omit such cases from now on.

A.1.2
(P,Q)=(2,5)

Using SnapPy, the gluing data of the torus knot can be obtained by the following input:

\displaystyle\mathtt{Manifold(^{\prime}5\_1^{\prime}).gluing\_equations\_pgl()}

The torus knot complement can be ideally triangulated with
r=3
tetrahedra with following gluing data

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{1}=Z_{1}+Z_{3},\quad C_{2}=2Z^{\prime}_{1}+Z^{\prime\prime}_{1}+Z_{2}+2Z_{2}^{\prime\prime}+Z^{\prime}_{3}+2Z_{3}^{\prime\prime},\\
&C_{3}=Z_{1}+Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{2}+2Z_{2}^{\prime}+Z_{3}+Z_{3}^{\prime},\quad M=-Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime}+Z_{3}^{\prime\prime}\;.\end{split}

(250)

In the ideal triangulation, there is an easy internal edge
\{E_{I}\}=\{C_{1}\}
and we choose
\{H_{I}\}=\{C_{2}\}
.

Among the
3^{3}
possible polarization choices, there are six choices satisfying the positivity condition

\displaystyle B^{-1}A>0,\textrm{ or }({\mathbb{I}}-B^{-1}A)>0\;,

while all other choices violate this condition. We list some representative cases among them, as well as for the other examples below.

i)

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z^{\prime\prime}_{1},Z_{2},Z^{\prime\prime}_{3})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z^{\prime}_{1},Z_{2}^{\prime\prime},Z^{\prime}_{3})^{T}\;,

(251)

the NZ matrices are

\displaystyle A=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-1&1&1\\
1&1&2\\
-1&0&-1\end{array}\right),\quad B=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&1&0\\
2&2&1\\
-1&0&-1\end{array}\right),\quad{\bm{\nu}}_{x}=(-1,-2,0)^{T}\;.

In the case,
B^{-1}A\in M_{3}(\mathbb{Z})
and
|\det B|=1
and thus we can choose
\mathbf{Q}=\mathbb{I}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T[S^{3}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{(2,5)}]=\mathbb{T}[A,B;\mathbf{Q}]\\
&=U(1)^{3}_{K-\mathbb{I}/2}\textrm{ coupled to }3\;\Phi\textrm{s of charge }\mathbf{Q}=\mathbb{I}\textrm{ with }\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}\\
&\quad\;\;\textrm{with }K=B^{-1}A=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}2&-1&-1\\
-1&1&1\\
-1&1&2\\
\end{array}\right)\textrm{ and }{\cal O}_{E_{1}}=V_{(1,0,1)}\;.\end{split}

(258)

The affine spaces
\mathfrak{M}[\mathcal{T}]
in (
106
) and vector space in
\mathfrak{F}[\mathcal{T}]
(
47
) are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathfrak{M}[\mathcal{T}]=\{\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})^{T}\;:\;\mu_{1}+\mu_{3}=0\}\;,\\
&\mathfrak{F}[\mathcal{T}]=\{\bm{v}=(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})^{T}\;:\;v_{1}+v_{3}=0\}\;.\end{split}

(259)

As expected in (
107
) and (
49
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}=B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}=(0,-1,0)^{T}\in\mathfrak{M}[\mathcal{T}]\;,\\
&\mathfrak{F}[\mathcal{T}]=\textrm{Span}\{\bm{v}_{m}:=B^{-1}\cdot(1,0,0)^{T}=(-2,1,2)^{T},\;\bm{v}_{1}:=B^{-1}\cdot(0,1,0)^{T}=(1,0,-1)^{T}\}\;.\end{split}

(260)

The superconformal index (
114
) for the theory is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,5)}]}_{\rm sci}[u,\eta;\mu_{m},\nu]={\cal I}^{T[A,B]}_{\rm sci}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]\big|_{*}\textrm{ where }\\
&{\cal I}^{T[A,B]}_{\rm sci}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\oint\frac{du_{i}}{2\pi iu_{i}}\right)u_{1}^{2m_{1}-m_{2}-m_{3}}u_{2}^{-m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}}u_{3}^{-m_{1}+m_{2}+2m_{3}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\prod_{i=1}^{3}(x_{i}(-q^{1/2})^{\mu_{i}})^{m_{i}}{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m_{i},u_{i})\;,\\
&\big|_{*}\;:\;(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\rightarrow\left(\frac{1}{u^{2}\eta},u,\frac{u^{2}}{\eta}\right),\;\;(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})\rightarrow(-2\mu_{m}-\nu,-1+\mu_{m},2\mu_{m}-\nu)\;.\end{split}

(261)

Using the superconformal index, one can explicitly verify that the
U(1)_{m}
symmetry
decouples in the IR — the dependence on
(u,\mu_{m})
identically drops out:

\displaystyle{\cal I}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,5)}]}_{\rm sci}[u,\eta;\mu_{m},\nu]={\cal I}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,5)}]}_{\rm sci}[\eta;\nu]\;.

(262)

In
q
-expansion,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,5)}]}[\eta;\nu]=1+(\eta-1)q+\left(\eta+\frac{1}{\eta}-2\right)q^{2}+\left(\eta+\frac{1}{\eta}-2\right)q^{3}\\
&+\left(\eta^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta}-2\right)q^{4}+(\eta^{2}-\eta)q^{5}+\left(2\eta^{2}-3\eta-\frac{1}{\eta}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+1\right)q^{6}+\ldots\bigg|_{\eta\rightarrow\eta(-q^{1/2})^{\nu}}\;.\end{split}

(263)

At
\nu_{a}=0
and
\nu_{b}=2
, as anticipated in (
123
), the index becomes

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,5)}]}[\eta=1;\nu=0]={\cal I}_{\rm sci}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,5)}]}[\eta=1;\nu=2]=1\;.

(264)

The next ones to be considered are the twisted partition functions. At the A-twisting point, i.e.
\bm{\mu}=\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}=(0,-1,0)^{T}
and
\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0}
, the
{\cal W}_{0}
and
{\cal W}_{1}
in (
165
) becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal W}^{\rm a}_{0}\simeq Z_{1}^{2}-Z_{1}Z_{2}+\frac{Z_{2}^{2}}{2}-Z_{1}Z_{3}+Z_{2}Z_{3}+Z_{3}^{2}-i\pi Z_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}{\rm Li}_{2}(e^{-Z_{i}})\;,\\
&{\cal W}^{\rm a}_{1}\simeq-\frac{1}{2}\left(Z_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\log(1-e^{-Z_{i}})\right)\;.\end{split}

(265)

Then,
\mathbf{HF}
at the point is

\displaystyle\mathbf{HF}_{\rm a}\simeq\bigg\{\left(\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{2},1\right),\;\left(\frac{5+\sqrt{5}}{2},\mathbf{e}(\frac{1}{5})\right)\bigg\}\;.

(266)

At the B-twisting point, i.e.
\bm{\mu}=\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}+2\bm{v}_{1}=(2,-1,-2)^{T}
and
\bm{\zeta}=\mathbf{0}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal W}^{\rm b}_{0}\simeq Z_{1}^{2}-Z_{1}Z_{2}+\frac{Z_{2}^{2}}{2}-Z_{1}Z_{3}+Z_{2}Z_{3}+Z_{3}^{2}+i\pi(2Z_{1}-Z_{2}-2Z_{3})+\sum_{i=1}^{3}{\rm Li}_{2}(e^{-Z_{i}})\;,\\
&{\cal W}^{\rm b}_{1}\simeq-\frac{1}{2}\left(-2Z_{1}+Z_{2}+2Z_{3}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\log(1-e^{-Z_{i}})\right)\;.\end{split}

(267)

Then,
\mathbf{HF}
at the point is

\displaystyle\mathbf{HF}_{\rm b}\simeq\bigg\{\left(\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{2},1\right),\;\left(\frac{5+\sqrt{5}}{2},\mathbf{e}(-\frac{1}{5})\right)\bigg\}\;.

(268)

It matches the
\mathbf{HF}
of
{\cal M}(2,5)
.

The half-index in (
210
) at the A-twisting point is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal I}^{a}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}]&=\sum_{\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})^{T}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{m_{2}-\mu_{m}(-2m_{1}+m_{2}+2m_{3})}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}\\
&\simeq 1+q+q^{2}+q^{3}+2q^{4}+2q^{5}+3q^{6}+3q^{7}+4q^{8}+5q^{9}+\ldots\\
&=q^{1/60}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,5)}_{(1,2)}(q),\end{split}

(269)

The half-index in (
210
) at the B-twisting point is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal I}^{b}_{\rm half}[\mu_{m}\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}]&=\sum_{\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})^{T}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{-2m_{1}+m_{2}+2m_{3}-\mu_{m}(-2m_{1}+m_{2}+2m_{3})}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}\\
&\simeq 1+q^{2}+q^{3}+q^{4}+q^{5}+2q^{6}+2q^{7}+3q^{8}+3q^{9}+\ldots\\
&=q^{-11/60}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,5)}_{(1,1)}(q),\end{split}

(270)

ii)

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z^{\prime\prime}_{1},Z^{\prime}_{2},Z^{\prime}_{3})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z^{\prime}_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3})^{T}\;,

the NZ matrices are

\displaystyle A=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-1&-1&-1\\

-1&0&0\\
\end{array}\right),\quad B=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&-1\\

In the case,

. Therefore,

&=U(1)^{3}_{K-\mathbb{I}/2}\textrm{ coupled to }3\;\Phi\textrm{s of charge }\mathbf{Q}=\mathbb{I}\textrm{ with }\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}\\
&\quad\;\;\textrm{with }K=B^{-1}A=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}2&1&1\\
1&2&1\\
1&1&1\\
\end{array}\right)\textrm{ and }\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}=V_{(1,0,-1)}\;.\end{split}

(278)

{\mathfrak{M}}[{\cal T}]
,
{\mathfrak{F}}[{\cal T}]
,

,

, and

\begin{gathered}{\mathfrak{M}}[{\cal T}]=\{\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})^{T}:\,\mu_{1}-\mu_{3}=1\}\,,\quad{\mathfrak{F}}[{\cal T}]=\textrm{Span}\{\bm{v}_{m},\;\bm{v}_{1}\}\,,\\
\bm{\mu}_{\rm geo}=B^{-1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}=(0,0,-1)^{T}\,,\\
\bm{v}_{m}:=B^{-1}\cdot(1,0,0)^{T}=(-1,0,-1)^{T}\,,\quad\bm{v}_{1}:=B^{-1}\cdot(0,1,0)^{T}=(0,-1,0)^{T}\,,\\
\end{gathered}

(279)

The superconformal index (
114
) for the theory is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{T[S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(2,5)}]}_{\rm sci}[u,\eta;\mu_{m},\nu]={\cal I}^{T[A,B]}_{\rm sci}[\bm{x},\bm{\mu}]\big|_{*}\,,\,\textrm{ where }\\
&\big|_{*}\;:\;(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\rightarrow\left(\frac{1}{u},\frac{1}{\eta},\frac{1}{u}\right),\;\;(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3})\rightarrow(-\mu_{m},-\nu,-1-\mu_{m})\;.\end{split}

(280)

The
q
-expansion and properties of superconformal index (

) - (

) are identical to those in
i)
. We have verified that these properties hold for all examples; hence, we will omit the superconformal index analysis in what follows.

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3}^{\prime})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z_{1}^{\prime\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime\prime},Z_{3})^{T}\;,

the NZ matrices are

, and we can choose

The half-indices at the A/B-twisting points are

\displaystyle\overline{{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}^{a}}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]=\overline{{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}^{b}}[\mu_{m}\in 2\mathbb{Z}]=0

(286)

By taking the reduction procedure on the gluing equations in (

), we have

\displaystyle H_{1}^{\mathrm{(r)}}:=C_{2}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=Z_{2}+2Z_{2}^{\prime\prime}\;.

(287)

All 3 choices of polarization satisfy the positivity condition

\displaystyle(B^{-1}A)_{\mathrm{r}}>0\textrm{, or }({\mathbb{I}}-B^{-1}A)_{\mathrm{r}}>0\;.

reduced-i)

It is the Nahm-sum like expression (

) with

A.1.3
(P,Q)=(2,7)

Using the SnapPy, the gluing data of the torus knot can be obtained by the following input:

\displaystyle\mathtt{Manifold(^{\prime}7\_1^{\prime}).gluing\_equations\_pgl()}

The torus knot complement can be ideally triangulated with
r=4
tetrahedra with following gluing data

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{1}=2Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{2}^{\prime}+Z_{3}^{\prime}+Z_{4}^{\prime\prime},\quad C_{2}=Z_{2}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{4}^{\prime},\\
&C_{3}=2Z_{1}+Z_{1}^{\prime}+2Z_{2}+2Z_{3}+2Z_{4},\\
&C_{4}=Z_{1}^{\prime}+Z_{2}^{\prime}+Z_{2}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{3}^{\prime}+2Z_{3}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{4}^{\prime}+Z_{4}^{\prime\prime},\\
&M=Z_{2}^{\prime}+Z_{3}^{\prime\prime}-Z_{4}\;.\end{split}

(301)

In the ideal triangulation, there are two easy internal edges
\{E_{I}\}=\{C_{1},C_{2}\}
and we choose
\{H_{I}\}=\{C_{3}\}
.

Among the
3^{4}
possible polarization choices, there are four choices satisfying the positivity condition.

i)

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z_{1}^{\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime},Z_{3}^{\prime\prime},Z_{4}^{\prime\prime})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3}^{\prime},Z_{4}^{\prime})^{T}\;,

(302)

the NZ matrices are

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}0&1&1&1\\
1&0&-2&-2\\
-2&1&0&1\\
0&-1&0&0\end{pmatrix},\quad B=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&1\\
2&2&-2&-2\\
-2&0&1&0\\
0&-1&0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad{\bm{\nu}}_{x}=(-1,2,0,-1)^{T}\;.

(303)

In the case, since
B^{-1}A\notin M_{4}({\mathbb{Z}})
, we choose
\mathbf{Q}=\mathop{\rm diag}\nolimits(2,1,1,1)
. Then,

\begin{gathered}K=\begin{pmatrix}6&0&2&0\\

A.1.4
(P,Q)=(2,9)

There are two ways to obtain the gluing data of the torus knot using the SnapPy. The first way is using the Dowker-Thistlethwaite (DT) code:

\displaystyle\mathtt{Manifold(^{\prime}DT:[(10,12,14,16,18,2,4,6,8)]^{\prime}).gluing\_equations\_pgl()}

The torus knot complement can be ideally triangulated with
r=5
tetrahedra with following gluing data

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{1}=Z_{4}^{\prime}+Z_{5}^{\prime\prime},\quad C_{2}=Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{2}^{\prime}+Z_{3},\quad C_{3}=2Z_{1}^{\prime}+Z_{2}^{\prime\prime},\\
&C_{4}=2Z_{1}+Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{2}+2Z_{3}^{\prime}+2Z_{4}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{5}^{\prime},\\
&C_{5}=Z_{2}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{3}+2Z_{3}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{4}+Z_{4}^{\prime}+2Z_{5}+Z_{5}^{\prime\prime},\\
&M=2Z_{1}+Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{2}+Z_{3}^{\prime}-Z_{3}^{\prime\prime}-Z_{4}^{\prime}+Z_{4}^{\prime\prime}+Z_{5}^{\prime}\end{split}

(324)

In the ideal triangulation, there are three easy internal edges
\{E_{I}\}=\{C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\}
, and we choose
\{H_{I}\}=\{C_{4}\}
.

Among the
3^{5}
possible polarization choices, there are four choices satisfying the positivity condition.

i)

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=(Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3}^{\prime},Z_{4}^{\prime\prime},Z_{5}^{\prime})^{T}\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}=(Z_{1}^{\prime\prime},Z_{2}^{\prime\prime},Z_{3},Z_{4}^{\prime},Z_{5})^{T}\;,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&H_{1}^{\mathrm{(r)}}:=C_{4}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=2Z_{3}^{\prime}+2Z_{4}^{\prime}+Z_{5}+2Z_{5}^{\prime\prime},\\
&E_{1}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=C_{2}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=Z_{3}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{4}+Z_{5},\quad E_{2}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=C_{3}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=Z_{4}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{5}^{\prime}\;.\end{split}

(349)

Among the
3^{3}
possible polarization choices, there are four choices satisfying the positivity condition.

reduced-ii)

When we choose the following polarizations

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\mathrm{r}}=(Z_{3}^{\prime\prime},Z_{4}^{\prime\prime},Z_{5})\textrm{ and }\mathbf{P}_{\mathrm{r}}=(Z_{3}^{\prime},Z_{4}^{\prime},Z_{5}^{\prime\prime})\;,

(350)

the reduced NZ matrices are

\displaystyle A_{\mathrm{r}}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&1\\
1&-2&1\\
0&1&-2\end{pmatrix},\quad B_{\mathrm{r}}=\begin{pmatrix}2&2&2\\
0&-2&0\\
0&0&-2\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{\nu}_{x;\mathrm{r}}=(-2,0,0)^{T}\;.

(351)

In the case, we choose
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathrm{r}}=\mathop{\rm diag}\nolimits(2,2,1)
. Then,
N_{\mathrm{r}}=2
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K_{\mathrm{r}}=\begin{pmatrix}2&-2&0\\
-2&4&-1\\
0&-1&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\bm{\mu}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=(-2,0,0)^{T},\\
&\bm{v}_{1}^{\mathrm{(r)}}=(1,0,0)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(352)

The chiral primary operators corresponding to each easy internal edges are

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{E_{1}^{\mathrm{(r)}}}=\phi_{1}\phi_{3}V_{(0,1,0)},\quad{\cal O}_{E_{2}^{\mathrm{(r)}}}=\phi_{2}V_{(0,0,2)}\;.

(353)

\nu_{a}=0
and
\nu_{b}=2
. The half-indices at the A/B-twisting points are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}^{a;\mathrm{(r)}}&=1+q^{1/2}+3q^{3/2}+6q^{2}+7q^{5/2}+10q^{3}+18q^{7/2}+30q^{4}+\ldots\;,\\
{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}^{b;\mathrm{(r)}}&=1+2q^{1/2}+3q+7q^{3/2}+12q^{2}+19q^{5/2}+31q^{3}+48q^{7/2}+...\;.\end{split}

(354)

We could not identify these half-indices with previously known RCFT characters.
Instead, we find that they can be completed into a full set of eight characters

\{\chi_{\alpha}(q)\}_{\alpha=0}^{7}
given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\chi_{0}(q)&=q^{-1/72}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}=q^{-1/72}{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}^{a;\mathrm{(r)}}\,,\\
\chi_{1}(q)&=q^{41/72}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}\left({q^{m_{1}-2m_{2}}-q^{m_{1}}}\right),\\
\chi_{2}(q)&=q^{1/24}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}q^{-m_{2}},\\
\chi_{3}(q)&=q^{-7/72}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}q^{-m_{1}}=q^{-7/72}{\cal I}_{\mathrm{half}}^{b;\mathrm{(r)}}\,,\\
\chi_{4}(q)&=q^{11/72}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}\left({q^{-2m_{1}+m_{2}}-q^{m_{2}}}\right),\\
\chi_{5}(q)&=q^{-5/24}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}\left({q^{-m_{1}}-q^{m_{1}}}\right),\\
\chi_{6}(q)&=q^{-13/72}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}\left({q^{m_{2}}-q^{2m_{1}-m_{2}}-q^{m_{2}}+q^{2m_{1}+m_{2}}}\right),\\
\chi_{7}(q)&=q^{-19/72}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{3}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K_{\mathrm{r}}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{2m_{1}}}{(q)_{2m_{1}}(q)_{2m_{2}}(q)_{m_{3}}}\left({q^{-m_{1}+m_{2}}-q^{m_{1}+m_{2}}}\right)\;.\end{split}

(355)

These
q
-series satisfy the modular transformation property

\displaystyle\chi_{\alpha}\!\left(q=e^{-2\pi i/\tau}\right)=S_{\alpha\beta}\,\chi_{\beta}\!\left(q=e^{2\pi i\tau}\right),

(356)

with

\displaystyle S_{\alpha\beta}=(-1)^{\alpha\beta}\,\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}\,\sin\!\left(\frac{2(\alpha+1)(\beta+1)\pi}{9}\right).

(357)

We have numerically checked this modular relation for various values of
\tau

satisfying
|e^{2\pi i\tau}|<1
and
|e^{2\pi i(-1/\tau)}|<1
, and we expect that

\{\chi_{\alpha}(q)\}
arise as characters of a rational VOA.

A.1.5

r
tetrahedra with the following gluing data

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{1}=2Z_{1}^{\prime}+Z_{2}^{\prime\prime},\quad\left.C_{I}\right|_{2\leq I\leq r-3}=Z_{I-1}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{I}^{\prime}+Z_{I+1}^{\prime\prime},\quad C_{r-2}=Z_{r-1}^{\prime}+Z_{r}^{\prime\prime},\\
&C_{r-1}=2Z_{1}+Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=2}^{r-2}2Z_{i}+2Z_{r-1}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{r}^{\prime},\\
&C_{r}=Z_{r-3}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{r-2}^{\prime}+Z_{r-2}^{\prime\prime}+2Z_{r-1}+Z_{r-1}^{\prime}+2Z_{r}+Z_{r}^{\prime\prime},\\

&M=Z_{1}^{\prime}-Z_{1}^{\prime\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime}-Z_{2}^{\prime\prime}-2Z_{3}^{\prime}-Z_{3}^{\prime\prime}\;.\end{split}

.

{\cal M}(7,12)
admits a

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry generated by the Verlinde loop associated with the primary
(1,7)
; this is the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
by which we quotient in
{\cal M}(7,12)/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

{\cal M}(7,12)
also has a

\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{f}

(1,5)

\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{f}
generated by
(1,3)
.
The final quotient
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\rm diag}
is the diagonal subgroup of these two fermionic symmetries, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\rm diag}\;\subset\;\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{f}({\cal M}(7,12))\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{f}({\cal M}(3,4))\,,

(432)

generated by the simultaneous action of
(1,5)
in
{\cal M}(7,12)
and
(1,3)
in
{\cal M}(3,4)

Appendix B
Partition functions of
SL(2,\mathbb{C})
CS theory

k=1
(state-integral model)

When
k=1
and
\sigma=\frac{1-b^{2}}{1+b^{2}}
, the partition function on a knot complement
M=S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}
can be computed using the following finite-dimensional integral
Dimofte:2012qj
;
Dimofte:2014zga
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&Z^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=1}}_{M=S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}}[X_{m}]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\det B}}\int\frac{d^{r}\bm{Z}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{r/2}}\exp\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}^{T}B^{-1}A\mathbf{Z}}{2\hbar}+\frac{\mathbf{Z}^{T}B^{-1}(\mathbf{X}+(i\pi+\frac{\hbar}{2})\bm{\nu}_{x})}{\hbar}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{r}\psi_{\hbar}(Z_{i})\;,\\
&\mathbf{X}=(X_{m},0,\ldots,0)^{T}\;.\end{split}

(485)

The special function
\psi_{\hbar}(Z)
is called quantum dilogarithm (Q.D.L) function, which is defined by
Faddeev:1993rs

\displaystyle\psi_{\hbar}(Z):=\begin{cases}\prod_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1-q^{r}e^{-Z}}{1-\widetilde{q}^{-r+1}e^{-\widetilde{Z}}}\;,\quad\textrm{if $|q|<1$}\\
\prod_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1-\widetilde{q}^{r}e^{-\widetilde{Z}}}{1-q^{-r+1}e^{-Z}}\;,\quad\textrm{if $|q|>1$}\end{cases}

(486)

with

\displaystyle q=e^{2\pi ib^{2}},\quad\widetilde{q}:=e^{2\pi ib^{-2}},\quad\widetilde{Z}=Z/b^{2}\;.

(487)

In the state-integral model, we omit the overall factor of the form

\displaystyle\exp\left(\big(\frac{\pi^{2}}{6\hbar}-\frac{\hbar}{24}\big)\mathbb{Z}+\frac{i\pi}{4}\mathbb{Z}+\frac{(2\pi i+\hbar)X_{m}}{\hbar}\mathbb{Q}+\frac{X_{m}^{2}}{\hbar}\mathbb{Q}\right)\;.

(488)

In the 3D-3D relation (
9
), such overall factors can be interpreted as contributions from the gravitational Chern–Simons term and from mixed Chern–Simons terms for the background gauge fields coupled to the
U(1)_{R}
and
U(1)_{m}
symmetries of the
T[M]
theory. Because these background CS terms do not affect the IR dynamics of
T[M]
theory and can be ignored.

k=0
(3D index)

When
k=0
, the complex Chern–Simons partition function produces a quantum invariant known as the 3D index,
{\cal I}^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=0}}_{S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}}(m,e;q)

Dimofte:2011py
. To define the 3D index from an ideal triangulation of
S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}
, we need the expressions for the internal edges
\{C_{I}\}_{I=1}^{r}
, the meridian variable
M
, and the longitude variable
L
in terms of the edge variables, as in (
16
) for the
C_{I}
and
M
variables. In addition to the symplectic structure among
\{C_{I}\}
and
M
given in (
18
), the longitude variable
L
satisfies the following Poisson brackets:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{L,C_{I}\}_{\rm P.B.}=0,\qquad\forall\,I=1,\ldots,r,\\[4.0pt]
&\{M,L\}_{\rm P.B.}=2\;.\end{split}

(489)

Using the gluing data and a choice of polarization
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{P})
, one can introduce an
Sp(2r,\mathbb{Z})
matrix

g=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix},

together with
r
-vectors
\bm{\nu}_{x}
and
\bm{\nu}_{p}
, defined through the relation

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}M\\[4.0pt]
C_{1}-2\pi i\\
\vdots\\
C_{r-1}-2\pi i\\[2.0pt]
\hline\cr L/2\\[2.0pt]
\Gamma_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\Gamma_{r-1}\end{array}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{P}\end{pmatrix}+i\pi\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\nu}_{x}\\
\bm{\nu}_{p}\end{array}\right)\;.

Using the relations in the affine gluing space (
21
), the variable
L/2
can always be rewritten as a linear combination of the edge variables with integer coefficients.
The variables
\{\Gamma_{I}\}_{I=1}^{r-1}
are the conjugate variables to
\{C_{I}\}_{I=1}^{r-1}
, satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{C_{I},\Gamma_{J}\}_{\rm P.B.}=\delta_{IJ},\qquad\{\Gamma_{I},\Gamma_{J}\}_{\rm P.B.}=0,\qquad\{M,\Gamma_{I}\}_{\rm P.B.}=\{L,\Gamma_{I}\}_{\rm P.B.}=0,\\
&\forall I,J=1,\ldots,r-1\;.\end{split}

(500)

Using the extended NZ data
(g,\bm{\nu}_{x},\bm{\nu}_{p})
, the 3D index in charge basis is defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=0}}_{S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}}(m,e;q)\\
&:=\sum_{(e_{2},\ldots,e_{r})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r-1}}\left((-q^{1/2})^{\mathbf{m}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{p}-\mathbf{e}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{x}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}{\cal I}_{\Delta}\!\left((g^{-1}\bm{\gamma})_{i},\,(g^{-1}\bm{\gamma})_{r+i}\right)\right)\bigg|_{\;m_{1}\to m,\;e_{1}\to e,\;m_{I>1}\to 0}\\[4.0pt]
&\text{where}\quad\mathbf{m}:=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r})^{T},\quad\mathbf{e}:=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{r})^{T},\quad\bm{\gamma}:=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{r},\,e_{1},\ldots,e_{r})^{T}\;.\end{split}

(501)

Here the tetrahedron index
{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m,e)
is defined as
Dimofte:2011py

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m,u):=\prod_{r=0}^{\infty}\frac{1-q^{r-\frac{1}{2}m+1}u^{-1}}{1-q^{r-\frac{1}{2}m}u}=\sum_{e\in\mathbb{Z}}{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m,e)u^{e},\;\;\\
&\textrm{where }{\cal I}_{\Delta}(m,e)=\sum_{n=\lfloor e\rfloor}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}q^{\frac{1}{2}n(n+1)-(n+\frac{1}{2}e)m}}{(q)_{n}(q)_{n+e}}\;.\end{split}

(502)

The 3D-index in fugacity basis is defined as

\displaystyle{\cal I}^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=0}}_{S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}}(m,u;q):=\sum_{e\in\mathbb{Z}}{\cal I}^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=0}}_{S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}}(m,e;q)u^{e}\;.

(503)

For the case in which
M=S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}
is the complement of the

(P,Q)
torus knot
{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}
, the 3D index takes a remarkably simple form
Celoria:2025lqm
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\cal I}^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=0}}_{S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}}(m,e;q)=\delta_{\,m+\frac{PQ}{2},\,0}\;,\;\;\text{(in charge basis)}\\
&{\cal I}^{SL(2,\mathbb{C})_{k=0}}_{S^{3}\backslash{\cal K}_{(P,Q)}}(m,u;q)=u^{-\frac{PQ}{2}m}\;,\;\;\text{(in fugacity basis)}\end{split}

(504)

Appendix C
Characters and Modular data of simple rational VOAs

Virasoro Minimal model
{\cal M}(P,Q)

The integers
P
and
Q
labeling
{\cal M}(P,Q)
should be coprime.
A primary operator of
{\cal M}(P,Q)
is labeled by two integers
1\leq r<P
and
1\leq r<Q
, and its character is

\begin{gathered}\chi^{M(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}=\chi^{M(P,Q)}_{(P-r,Q-s)}=\frac{q^{\Delta^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}}}{(q)_{\infty}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(q^{n^{2}PQ+n(Qr-Ps)}-q^{(nP+r)(nQ+s)}\right)\;,\\
h^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}=\frac{(Qr-Ps)^{2}-(P-Q)^{2}}{4PQ}\;,\quad c(P,Q)=1-\frac{6(P-Q)^{2}}{PQ}\,,\\
\Delta^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}=h^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}-\frac{1}{24}c(P,Q)\,.\end{gathered}

(505)

The
S
-matrix of
{\cal M}(P,Q)
is

S^{M(P,Q)}_{(r,s),{(r^{\prime},s^{\prime})}}=\sqrt{\frac{8}{PQ}}(-1)^{1+rs^{\prime}+sr^{\prime}}\sin\left({\pi\frac{Q}{P}rr^{\prime}}\right)\sin\left({\pi\frac{P}{Q}ss^{\prime}}\right)\,.

(506)

Supersymmetric Virasoro Minimal model
SM(P,Q)

The integers
P
and
Q
labeling
SM(P,Q)
should satisfy two conditions:

\displaystyle P\equiv Q\textrm{ (mod 2)}\,,\quad{\rm gcd}(P,\frac{P-Q}{2})=1\,.

(507)

Again a primary operator of
SM(P,Q)
is labeled by two integers
1\leq r<P
and
1\leq r<Q
.
(r,s)
where
r-s\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}
corresponds to NS sector, and otherwise R sector. The character is

\begin{gathered}\chi^{SM(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}=q^{\Delta^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}}\frac{(-q^{t};q)_{\infty}}{(q)_{\infty}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(q^{(n^{2}PQ+n(Qr-Ps))/2}-q^{(nP+r)(nQ+s)/2}\right)\,,\\
h^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}=\frac{(Qr-Ps)^{2}-(P-Q)^{2}}{8PQ}+\frac{2t-1}{16}\,,\quad c(P,Q)=\frac{3}{2}\left(1-\frac{2(P-Q)^{2}}{PQ}\right)\,,\\
\Delta^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}=h^{(P,Q)}_{(r,s)}-\frac{1}{24}c(P,Q)\,,\quad t=\begin{cases}1/2,&r-s\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\\
1,&r-s\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}+1\,.\end{cases}\end{gathered}

(508)

The
S
-matrix for the NS characters of
SM(P,Q)
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{SM(P,Q)}_{(r,s),(r^{\prime},s^{\prime})}&=\frac{2}{\sqrt{PQ}}\biggr(\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2PQ}(Ps-Qr)(Ps^{\prime}-Qr^{\prime})\right)\\
&\quad-\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2PQ}(Ps+Qr)(Ps^{\prime}-Qr^{\prime})\right)\biggr)\,.\end{split}

(509)

Nahm–sum like expressions

We review the known Nahm–sum like expressions for the characters of minimal models. It is known that a large class of
2d
RCFT characters can be written as fermionic sum, or
Nahm sum formula

Nahm:2004ch
;
Zagier:2007knq
;
Berkovich_1996
;
melzer1994susyanalogGAid
;
Bytsko_1999
;
Kedem:1993ze
;
Berkovich:1994es
;
welsh2005fermionic
:

\chi(q)=\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}m^{T}Am+B^{T}m+C}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}\cdots(q)_{m_{k}}}\,,

(510)

where
A
,
B
, and
C
are some
k\times k
matrix, length-
k
vector, and scalar each.

The characters of
{\cal M}(2,2k+3)
can be represented exactly as the form of (
510
)
melzer1994susyanalogGAid
:

\begin{gathered}\chi^{{\cal M}(2,2k+3)}_{(1,s)}(q)=q^{\Delta^{(2,2k+3)}_{(1,s)}}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{k}_{\geq 0}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}m^{T}Am+B^{T}m}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}\cdots(q)_{m_{k}}}\,\quad(1\leq s\leq k+1),\\
\textrm{where }(A)_{ij}=2\,\mathrm{min}(i,j),\quad B=(\mathbf{0}_{s-1},\,1,\,2,\,\cdots,\,k-s+1)^{T}\,.\end{gathered}

(511)

For
{\cal M}(3,l)

Bytsko_1999
,

\displaystyle\chi^{{\cal M}(3,l)}_{(1,s)}(q)=q^{\Delta^{(3,l)}_{(1,s)}}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{k}_{\geq 0}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}m^{T}A^{(l)}m+m^{T}B^{(l)}_{s}}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}\cdots(q)_{m_{k-1}}(q)_{2m_{k}}}\,,

(512)

where
A^{(l)}
and
B^{(l)}
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}(A^{(3k+1)})_{ij}&=(A^{(3k+1)})_{ji}=2\min(i,j)\,,\qquad 1\leq i,j\leq k-1\,,\\
(A^{(3k+1)})_{jk}&=(A^{(3k+1)})_{kj}=2j+(1-k)\,\delta_{jk}\,,\qquad 1\leq j\leq k-1\,,\\
(A^{(3k+1)})_{kk}&=2k+2\,,\\
(A^{(3k+2)})_{ij}&=(A^{(3k+1)})_{ij}-2\,\delta_{ik}\delta_{jk}\,,\\
\end{split}

(513)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}(B^{(3k+1)}_{s})_{j}&=\max(j-s+1,0)+(j-k-1)\,\delta_{jk}\,,\\
(B^{(3k+2)}_{s})_{j}&=(B^{(3k+1)}_{s})_{j}+\,\delta_{jk}\,.\end{split}

(514)

Abelian Wess-Zumino-Witten model

For a Wess-Zumino-Witten (WZW) model with gauge group
U(1)^{r}
and level matrix
K\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}
, there are
|\det K|
primary operators
{\cal O}_{\bm{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}}
with the following equivalence relation

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{\bm{\alpha}}\sim{\cal O}_{\bm{\alpha}+K\cdot{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}}\;.

(515)

The central charge of
U(1)^{r}_{K}
WZW model and the conformal weight of primary operator
{\cal O}_{\bm{\alpha}}
are
Delmastro:2019vnj

\displaystyle c=r,\quad h_{\bm{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{2}\bm{\alpha}^{T}\cdot K^{-1}\cdot\bm{\alpha}\;.

(516)

The character is

\displaystyle\chi_{\bm{\alpha}}^{U(1)^{r}_{K}}(q)=\frac{q^{h_{\bm{\alpha}}-c/24}}{(q)_{\infty}^{r}}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}}q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot K\cdot\mathbf{m}-\bm{\alpha}^{T}\cdot\mathbf{m}}\;.

(517)

The modular
S
-matrix is

\displaystyle S_{\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det K|}}\exp\left({2\pi i\bm{\alpha}^{T}\cdot K^{-1}\cdot\bm{\beta}}\right)\;.

(518)

For
r=1
, there is Nahm–sum like expression of the character
Gang:2024loa

\displaystyle\chi_{\alpha}^{U(1)_{k}}(q)=q^{h_{\alpha}-1/24}\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{2}}\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{m}^{T}\cdot J_{k}\cdot\mathbf{m}}(-q^{1/2})^{-2\alpha(1,-1)\cdot\mathbf{m}}}{(q)_{m_{1}}(q)_{m_{2}}}\;,

(519)

where

\displaystyle J_{k}=\begin{pmatrix}k&1-k\\
1-k&k\end{pmatrix}\;.

(520)

Free fermionic RCFT

The central charge of the free fermionic RCFT is
\frac{1}{2}
, and its character is
DiFrancesco:1997nk

\displaystyle\chi_{F}(q)=q^{-1/48}(-q^{1/2};q)_{\infty}\;.

(521)

\chi_{F}(q)
is a modular form itself.
